\title{Differential Splicing from Equivalence-Class Counts}

\section*{Overview}

\subsection*{Questions and unit of analysis}

The analysis asks two distinct questions:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
  \item Does local splice-path usage differ by experimental condition within a cell type?
  \item Does local splice-path usage differ across cell types within the same subjects?
\end{enumerate}
The primary analysis aggregates cells into \emph{subject-by-condition-by-cell-type pseudobulks} and estimates local splice-path compositions from their observed paired-primer equivalence-class (EC) counts. Biological replication is represented by subject; cells are never treated as independent condition replicates.

A splice block is a local region between constitutive exonic anchors. Transcripts that take the same path through that region are collapsed to one subisoform. This targets local splicing choices, which can be identifiable even when the corresponding full-length isoforms are not.

For condition effects, each hypothesis is indexed by a splice block and a cell type. It is an omnibus test of \[
  H_0:\quad\text{mean path usage is equal across the included conditions}.
\]
Only conditions represented by at least three subject pseudobulks are included. With two conditions and two paths, this is a subject-level differential path-log-ratio test. With more conditions or paths, all condition coefficients and path log-ratios are tested jointly.

For cell-type effects, the primary hypothesis is indexed by a nonredundant splice-path partition and jointly tests whether path usage differs among the represented cell types after blocking by subject. Pairwise subject-matched tests are secondary analyses used to localize omnibus signals and to match the hypotheses available from external comparators. A comparator pair must be represented in at least four subjects. Condition tests require at least 10 modeled gene UMIs per retained pseudobulk; the comparator benchmark uses 25 UMIs for alignment with the external-method screens. The cell-type and condition hypothesis families are reported separately.

\subsection*{Workflow}

The primary analysis proceeds in five steps:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
  \item Construct local splice blocks from the isoform annotation and map every supported isoform to a local path.
  \item Aggregate paired oligo(dT) and random-hexamer EC counts within each subject, condition, and cell type while retaining separate primer maps.
  \item For each block, construct a label-independent pooled isoform baseline and estimate only its $d_b=S_b-1$ local path-log-ratio perturbations while preserving within-path isoform mixtures and nuisance isoforms.
  \item Stabilize local path estimates with two pseudo-observations per path, then fit a subject-blocked omnibus cell-type model or a condition model within one cell type.
  \item Calibrate the resulting statistics using \(R_{\rm null}\) design-matched Monte Carlo null replicates with a leave-one-event-out empirical null CDF, then apply BH separately to cell-type and condition hypotheses. The default is \(R_{\rm null}=32\).
\end{enumerate}

The same Tealeaf local-path estimator is used for the comparator benchmark and full-data analyses. It retains both primer-specific EC observations and estimates the identifiable local path coordinates directly. Supporting and deprecated analyses appear in the appendices.

\section*{Notation and Dimensions}

An \emph{equivalence class} (EC) is a set of isoforms compatible with one UMI. An EC count is therefore observed, whereas its allocation among compatible isoforms is latent. A \emph{splice path} is an ordered local exon chain between two constitutive anchors. A \emph{subisoform} is the set of full-length isoforms taking the same path through one block. PSI means percent spliced in; for two paths it is one component of the path-composition vector. ILR means isometric log-ratio, an orthonormal coordinate system for a composition.

Let \(g=g(b)\) denote the gene containing block \(b\), and let \(\mathcal I_g\) be the set of isoforms retained by the paired-primer model and assigned to gene \(g\) by the isoform-to-gene table. Define \(T_g=|\mathcal I_g|\in\mathbb N\). This is a gene-level set, not the set of isoforms whose exons overlap the block, and it is therefore shared by every block of the gene. Let \(\mathcal I_b^{\rm sp}\subseteq\mathcal I_g\) be the retained, annotated spliced isoforms represented in the block's GTF-derived path map. Isoforms in \(\mathcal I_g\setminus\mathcal I_b^{\rm sp}\), including modeled unspliced precursors, remain nuisance isoforms in the EC likelihood.

Let \(I_b,S_b\in\mathbb N\) be the numbers of qualifying pseudobulks and represented spliced paths for block \(b\), with \(S_b\geq2\). Then \(d_b=S_b-1\in\mathbb N\) is its number of independent path contrasts. There are \(P=2\) primer protocols, and primer \(p\) has \(K_{gp}\in\mathbb N\) ECs for gene \(g\). The principal symbols in the primary model are listed below. \[
\begin{array}{lll}
\toprule
\text{symbol} & \text{dimension or domain} & \text{meaning}\\
\midrule
b & \{1,\ldots,\mathcal B\} & \text{splice-block index}\\
i & \{1,\ldots,I_b\} & \text{pseudobulk index within block }b\\
u & \{1,\ldots,U_b\} & \text{subject index within block }b\\
c & \{1,\ldots,C_b^{\rm cell}\} & \text{cell-type index within block }b\\
p & \{1,\ldots,P\} & \text{primer-protocol index}\\
t & \{1,\ldots,T_g\} & \text{isoform index within gene }g(b)\\
s & \{1,\ldots,S_b\} & \text{splice-path index}\\
\bm c_{bucp} & \mathbb N_0^{K_{gp}} & \text{observed subject--cell-type EC counts}\\
A_{gp} & \mathbb R_+^{K_{gp}\times T_g} & \text{weighted EC-by-isoform map}\\
\overline{\bm\theta}_g & \Delta^{T_g-1} & \text{label-independent pooled isoform baseline}\\
\bm\delta_{bi} & \mathbb R^{d_b} & \text{local path-log-ratio perturbation}\\
\bm\psi_{bi} & \Delta^{S_b-1} & \text{represented local path composition}\\
\bm y_{bi} & \mathbb R^{d_b} & \text{ILR coordinates of }\bm\psi_{bi}\\
Q_b & \mathbb R^{S_b\times d_b} & \text{Helmert ILR basis}\\
\bottomrule
\end{array}
\]
Here \(\mathcal B\in\mathbb N\) is the number of blocks, \(\mathbb N_0=\{0,1,\ldots\}\), and \(\Delta^{S_b-1}=\{\bm v\in\mathbb R_+^{S_b}:\bm1^\prime\bm v=1\}\). Bold lower-case symbols are vectors, upper-case symbols are matrices, \(\bm1_k\in\mathbb R^k\) is a vector of ones, \(\bm0_k\in\mathbb R^k\) is a vector of zeros, and \(I_k\in\mathbb R^{k\times k}\) is the identity.

\subsection*{Helmert basis and ILR coordinates}

The simplex has only \(d_b=S_b-1\) free directions because path proportions sum to one. The Helmert matrix \(Q_b\) is a fixed orthonormal basis for the sum-zero subspace: \[
 Q_b^\prime Q_b=I_{d_b},\qquad Q_b^\prime\bm1_{S_b}=\bm0_{d_b}.
\]
Using one-based row \(r\in\{1,\ldots,S_b\}\) and column \(j\in\{1,\ldots,d_b\}\), its entries are \[
 (Q_b)_{rj}=
 \begin{cases}
  1/\sqrt{j(j+1)}, & r\leq j,\\
  -j/\sqrt{j(j+1)}, & r=j+1,\\
  0, & r>j+1.
 \end{cases}
\]
For any strictly positive \(\bm\psi_{bi}\in\Delta^{S_b-1}\), the ILR coordinate is \(\bm y_{bi}=Q_b^\prime\log\bm\psi_{bi}\in\mathbb R^{d_b}\), where the logarithm is elementwise. For \(S_b=2\), \(Q_b=(1,-1)^\prime/\sqrt2\) and \(y_{bi}=\log(\psi_{bi1}/\psi_{bi2})/\sqrt2\). Thus a two-path ILR test is a scaled log-odds, or differential-PSI, test.

\section*{Methods}

\subsection*{Tealeaf}

\subsubsection*{Local-path estimation}

Fix a gene \(g\) and one of its splice blocks \(b\). For subject \(u\), cell-type level \(c\), and primer protocol \(p\in\{1,2\}\), let \(\bm c_{bucp}\in\mathbb N_0^{K_{gp}}\) be the observed EC-count vector and let \(A_{gp}\in\mathbb R_+^{K_{gp}\times T_g}\) be the fixed EC-by-isoform compatibility or conditional-weight matrix. Oligo(dT) and random-hexamer observations have separate count vectors and matrices, so primer-specific positional coverage enters the likelihood without changing the underlying isoform composition. Counts from the same subject and cell type are summed within primer. Conditioning on each observed primer total removes library size from the composition likelihood. An EC may be compatible with multiple isoforms; its count is not first allocated to a transcript or path. Unsupported isoforms and ECs not assigned to \(g\) are excluded, but every supported isoform of \(g\), including an unspliced nuisance isoform, remains in every block likelihood for that gene.

After defining the observations eligible for a test, the baseline is estimated from their EC counts and compatibility matrices without using the labels within that set. Let \(\mathcal I_b\) index the subject--cell-type observations included for block \(b\), and define the primer-specific pooled count vector \(\overline{\bm c}_{bp}=\sum_{(u,c)\in\mathcal I_b}\bm c_{bucp}\in\mathbb N_0^{K_{gp}}\). The label-independent pooled isoform composition \(\overline{\bm\theta}_g\in\Delta^{T_g-1}\) is the joint conditional-multinomial maximum-likelihood estimate \[\overline{\bm\theta}_g=\arg\max_{\bm\theta\in\Delta^{T_g-1}}\sum_{p=1}^2\overline{\bm c}_{bp}^{\mathsf T}\log\left(\frac{A_{gp}\bm\theta}{\bm1^{\mathsf T}A_{gp}\bm\theta}\right).\] Dependence on \(\mathcal I_b\) is suppressed in the notation. The implementation uses reference-logit coordinates, starts from equal isoform weights, and optimizes this low-dimensional objective by L-BFGS. Primer counts are pooled separately, so their distinct compatibility and positional-coverage maps remain in the objective, while conditioning on each primer total removes library-size differences. The estimate is computed once for each gene and included observation set and reused across blocks sharing both; because neither observed nor permuted labels enter its estimation, it is unchanged in the matched null analyses.

Let \(Q_b\in\mathbb R^{S_b\times d_b}\) be the Helmert basis defined above. Let \(r_b(t)\in\{1,\ldots,S_b\}\cup\{\varnothing\}\) map isoform \(t\) to its represented path or mark it as a nuisance isoform, let \(\mathcal R_b=\{t:r_b(t)\ne\varnothing\}\), and let \(\overline\rho_b=\sum_{t\in\mathcal R_b}\overline\theta_{gt}\in(0,1]\) be the pooled mass of represented isoforms. Thus \(\overline{\bm\theta}_g\) supplies the fixed within-path isoform ratios, nuisance-isoform abundances, and represented mass around which each subject--cell-type local path fit is perturbed.

For any \(\bm\delta\in\mathbb R^{d_b}\), define the perturbed isoform composition \(\bm\theta_g(\bm\delta)\in\Delta^{T_g-1}\) componentwise by \[\theta_{gt}(\bm\delta)=\begin{cases}\displaystyle \overline\rho_b\frac{\overline\theta_{gt}\exp\{Q_{b,r_b(t)}^{\mathsf T}\bm\delta\}}{\sum_{v\in\mathcal R_b}\overline\theta_{gv}\exp\{Q_{b,r_b(v)}^{\mathsf T}\bm\delta\}},&t\in\mathcal R_b,\\ \overline\theta_{gt},&t\notin\mathcal R_b.\end{cases}\] Define its represented path composition \(\bm\psi_b(\bm\delta)\in\Delta^{S_b-1}\) by \[\psi_{bs}(\bm\delta)=\frac{1}{\overline\rho_b}\sum_{t\in\mathcal R_b:r_b(t)=s}\theta_{gt}(\bm\delta),\qquad s\in\{1,\ldots,S_b\}.\] Thus \(\bm\delta\) changes only local path usage, while within-path isoform ratios, nuisance isoforms, and total represented mass remain fixed.

For subject--cell-type combination \((u,c)\), Tealeaf maximizes the two-primer EC log likelihood with a uniform fixed-total path concentration \(\zeta\geq0\), \[\widehat{\bm\delta}_{buc}(\zeta)=\arg\max_{\bm\delta\in\mathbb R^{d_b}}\left\{\sum_{p=1}^2\bm c_{bucp}^{\mathsf T}\log\left(\frac{A_{gp}\bm\theta_g(\bm\delta)}{\bm1^{\mathsf T}A_{gp}\bm\theta_g(\bm\delta)}\right)+\frac{\zeta}{S_b}\sum_{s=1}^{S_b}\log\psi_{bs}(\bm\delta)\right\}.\] Each of the \(S_b\) paths therefore receives \(\zeta/S_b\) pseudo-observations, so the total concentration does not depend on the number of represented paths. The penalty stabilizes boundary estimates without shrinking subject--cell-type compositions toward a shared biological baseline. The global value of \(\zeta\) is selected by the testing-level empirical-Bayes procedure below. Junction, exonic, and ambiguous reads contribute through their compatibility patterns without first assigning reads to global isoforms.

\subsubsection*{Statistical testing}

Let \(\widehat{\bm y}_{buc}=Q_b^{\mathsf T}\log\widehat{\bm\psi}_{buc}\in\mathbb R^{d_b}\) be the estimated splice path usage ratios (make non-redundant via $Q_b$). The primary cell-type analysis uses every fitted subject--cell-type vector for a block in one multivariate regression. Let \(N_b^{\rm obs}\in\mathbb N\), \(U_b\in\mathbb N\), and \(C_b^{\rm cell}\in\mathbb N\) be its numbers of subject--cell-type observations, subjects, and cell types, and stack the responses as \(Y_b\in\mathbb R^{N_b^{\rm obs}\times d_b}\). The design \(X_b\in\mathbb R^{N_b^{\rm obs}\times(U_b+C_b^{\rm cell}-1)}\) contains an intercept, \(U_b-1\) subject indicators, and \(C_b^{\rm cell}-1\) cell-type indicators. The equal-weight model is \[Y_b=X_b\Gamma_b+E_b,\qquad E_{bn}\overset{\mathrm{iid}}\sim N(\bm0_{d_b},\tau_b^2I_{d_b}),\] where \(\Gamma_b\in\mathbb R^{(U_b+C_b^{\rm cell}-1)\times d_b}\) and \(\tau_b^2\geq0\). The joint Wald test sets the \((C_b^{\rm cell}-1)\times d_b\) cell-type coefficient block to zero and therefore has at most \((C_b^{\rm cell}-1)d_b\) degrees of freedom. Subject indicators preserve the repeated-measures design while producing one cell-type hypothesis per block. With zero conditional measurement covariance, the REML scalar-variance generalized least-squares model reduces exactly to multivariate ordinary least squares, and the implementation uses this closed form.

For a condition test within one cell type, the subject-level vectors enter an equal-weight multivariate regression with an intercept and \(C_b^{\rm cond}-1\) condition columns, where \(C_b^{\rm cond}\in\mathbb N\) is the number of represented conditions. An isotropic between-subject variance is fitted by REML under the null, and all condition-by-path coefficients are tested jointly.

The secondary pairwise cell-type analysis localizes differences and supplies Tealeaf hypotheses matched to junction-based comparators. For cell types 1 and 2, define the paired subject contrast \(\widehat{\bm d}_{bu}=\widehat{\bm y}_{bu2}-\widehat{\bm y}_{bu1}\in\mathbb R^{d_b}\). A paired \(t\) test is used when \(d_b=1\), and Hotelling's test is used when \(d_b>1\). Scalar sampling variances are moderated across blocks with a robust scaled-inverse-chi-squared prior, refitted inside every null family.

Let \(R_{\rm null}\in\mathbb N\) be the number of Monte Carlo null replicates, with default \(R_{\rm null}=32\). For every block and replicate, the null transformation is a within-subject cell-type-label permutation for the primary omnibus regression, a condition-label permutation for a condition contrast, or a paired subject sign flip for a pairwise cell-type contrast. Collecting replicate \(r\in\{1,\ldots,R_{\rm null}\}\) across all blocks gives one null p-value family on which the complete BH procedure can be audited. Calibration pools the null reference p-values across blocks only within matched tested-dimension and subject-count strata. If \(\widetilde p_b\in[0,1]\) is block \(b\)'s uncalibrated reference p-value, \(\widetilde p_{jr}^{(0)}\in[0,1]\) is block \(j\)'s null reference p-value in replicate \(r\), and \(\mathcal S_b\) is the set of blocks in its calibration stratum, the leave-one-block-out calibrated value is \[p_b=\frac{1+\sum_{j\in\mathcal S_b\setminus\{b\}}\sum_{r=1}^{R_{\rm null}}\mathbf1\{\widetilde p_{jr}^{(0)}\leq\widetilde p_b\}}{1+R_{\rm null}(|\mathcal S_b|-1)}.\] Thus the calibration CDF contains approximately \(R_{\rm null}|\mathcal S_b|\) values rather than only \(R_{\rm null}\), while the whole-family BH audit still contains only \(R_{\rm null}\) realizations. The default 32 is a computational choice rather than a theoretically distinguished value; sensitivity to 64 and 128 replicates is reported below. BH is applied separately to the cell-type and condition families. Supporting analyses of path-estimate uncertainty, alternative smoothing, and the deprecated global EC GLMM are reported in the appendix.

Tealeaf selects one global fixed-total concentration from \(\mathcal Z=\{2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256\}\) by empirical Bayes at the level of the calibrated tests. Let \(p_j(\zeta)\in[0,1]\) be test \(j\)'s calibrated p-value after fitting with candidate \(\zeta\in\mathcal Z\). For each candidate, the two-groups marginal density is \(f_\zeta(p)=\pi_{0,\zeta}+(1-\pi_{0,\zeta})a_\zeta p^{a_\zeta-1}\), where \(\pi_{0,\zeta}\in(0,1)\) is the null weight and \(a_\zeta\in(0,1)\) is the shape of a decreasing beta alternative. The parameters maximize log evidence, averaged first over the \(n_g\in\mathbb N\) tests from gene \(g\) and then over the \(G\in\mathbb N\) genes, and the selected concentration is \[\widehat\zeta=\arg\max_{\zeta\in\mathcal Z}\frac1G\sum_{g=1}^G\frac1{n_g}\sum_{j:g(j)=g}\log f_\zeta\{p_j(\zeta)\},\] where \(g(j)\) maps test \(j\) to its gene. Gene-level averaging prevents genes with more blocks or cell-type pairs from dominating selection. This target chooses smoothing for distinguishing the calibrated null/non-null mixture, uses the same uniform-centered estimator in selection and testing, and requires no fitting-data split. The whole selector, including calibration and estimation of \((\pi_{0,\zeta},a_\zeta)\), is rerun within each matched null family so the FDR audit includes hyperparameter selection. One concentration is selected per analysis family rather than imposed across distinct hypothesis families. The subject-split comparator benchmark selects \(\widehat\zeta=32\), while the full-data omnibus cell-type, pairwise cell-type, and condition families each independently select \(\widehat\zeta=64\). Supporting analyses of alternative smoothing targets, path-estimate uncertainty, and the deprecated global EC GLMM are reported in the appendix.

The selected \(\widehat\zeta\) is used for test statistics, null calibration, p-values, and discovery selection. Descriptive path proportions and effect sizes are refitted with a separate fixed reporting concentration \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) after the discovery set is fixed. This weak barrier stabilizes otherwise nonconvergent boundary fits while avoiding the compression induced by a concentration optimized for testing-level mixture evidence. The reporting refit cannot alter a test statistic, p-value, or discovery. Both concentrations are stated whenever fitted path usage is shown.

\subsubsection*{Independent long-read validation}

We evaluated directional replication in the P56 brain ScISOr-Seq2 data from \href{https://doi.org/10.1038/s41593-024-01616-4}{Joglekar et al.}, generated independently by the Tilgner laboratory. We used the authors' UMI-filtered annotated-transcript count matrix rather than treating individual reads as biological replicates. The source matrix contains two biological replicates within each of five brain regions. Eight Tealeaf cell types have a prespecified correspondence to its annotations: astrocytes, mature oligodendrocytes, cerebellar granule neurons, cortical excitatory neurons, dentate-gyrus excitatory neurons, CA excitatory neurons, thalamic excitatory neurons, and medium spiny neurons. Source transcript names are mapped to stable GENCODE transcript identifiers and then collapsed with the same block-to-path map used by Tealeaf. For astrocytes and oligodendrocytes, region-specific samples are pooled within replicate index; a sensitivity excluding both cross-region glial mappings is reported below.

Directional validation uses the secondary pairwise BH discoveries because an omnibus hypothesis has no single direction. For selected test \(j\) with \(S_j\in\mathbb N\) paths, let \(\bm\Delta_j^{\rm T}\in\mathbb R^{S_j}\) be the difference between the two Tealeaf mean path-composition vectors from the \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) refit, and let \(\bm\Delta_{jr}^{\rm L}\in\mathbb R^{S_j}\) be the corresponding difference in long-read transcript-UMI proportions for source replicate \(r\in\{1,2\}\). The pooled long-read effect is \(\bm\Delta_j^{\rm L}\), computed after summing the two replicate count vectors. A test replicates in pooled direction when \((\bm\Delta_j^{\rm T})^{\mathsf T}\bm\Delta_j^{\rm L}>0\), and it replicates in both biological replicates when \((\bm\Delta_j^{\rm T})^{\mathsf T}\bm\Delta_{jr}^{\rm L}>0\) for both \(r\). The pooled sign-flip null rate is \(1/2\). For the stricter endpoint, the conditional null is half the observed fraction for which the two source-replicate dot products have the same sign; this preserves their between-replicate concordance and randomizes only the orientation of the Tealeaf contrast. The primary coverage rule requires at least 20 mapped long-read UMIs in each pooled cell type; the stricter endpoint additionally requires at least 10 in each cell-type--replicate combination.

We apply the same directional validation to full-data BH discoveries from LeafCutter, MAJIQ Heterogen, and scQuint, and to a paired junction-CLR ablation refitted over every candidate intron group with BH applied within each cell-type contrast. For junction group \(j\) containing \(K_j\in\mathbb N\) junctions, its short-read effect \(\bm\Delta_j^{\rm J}\in\mathbb R^{K_j}\) is the difference between equal-pseudobulk mean junction-composition vectors. This common post-selection reporting estimator avoids comparing method-specific direction estimates. A source junction count is the sum of annotated-transcript UMIs over source transcripts containing that junction, and the long-read junction effect \(\bm\Delta_j^{\rm L,J}\in\mathbb R^{K_j}\) is formed from the corresponding proportions. The pooled criterion is \((\bm\Delta_j^{\rm J})^{\mathsf T}\bm\Delta_j^{\rm L,J}>0\), with the analogous replicate-resolved criterion. A junction group is eligible only if every tested junction is represented by at least one source annotated transcript and it passes the same depth rules used for Tealeaf. The MAJIQ build requires support in at least three pseudobulks and its PSI-coverage filter requires at least three reads in at least three bins; Heterogen then requires coverage in at least half the pseudobulks within each compared group. MAJIQ edges are restored to their local splicing variations before composition estimation.

\subsection*{Comparators}

SUPPA2 v2.4 supplies the annotated event definitions and transcript-TPM inclusion proportions. For the matched split comparison, we use its classical paired signed-rank test family with exact conditional tails, including tied absolute differences, rather than the earlier vectorized normal approximation. Missing pairs and zero differences are omitted from the rank statistic, tied absolute differences receive average ranks, and events require at least eight finite subject pairs. Exact testing assumes a symmetric paired null distribution. The upstream classical implementation calls SciPy's Wilcoxon test directly; our former always-normal approximation was not equivalent to that call in small samples. Paired-$t$, moderated paired-$t$, and transformed-PSI sensitivities are reported in the appendix. Within-contrast event BH values are retained for event-level reporting, but they are not a screening step in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}, which aggregates all eligible raw event p-values before gene-level correction.

In addition to the pooled SUPPA2 comparator, we run a primer-aware SUPPA2 comparator using Tealeaf's paired-primer EC preparation and compatibility blocks. For event \(e\), sample \(i\), and primer \(p\), expected included and excluded counts are obtained by applying the production theta EC allocations to the standard SUPPA2 transcript sets. A shared event logit is estimated with a primer offset and event-specific beta-binomial overdispersion, with both refit separately for full data and each subject fold. For the matched split comparison, the resulting event PSI values use the exact paired signed-rank test defined above; events with fewer than eight valid subject pairs are excluded. The separately archived full-data and unrestricted split audits retain the earlier normal approximation. Event-level BH values are descriptive and do not screen the matched gene-level analysis. For the matched comparison, event p-values are combined by Simes within each gene--cell-type pair and intersected with Tealeaf's eligible gene--pair keys in both folds, so the table reports Tealeaf and primer-aware SUPPA2 on the same hypothesis universe. The unrestricted primer-aware audit remains a separate sensitivity analysis. A raw EC-conditional allocation is evaluated as an ablation, not as the production estimator.

The same biological subjects are divided into two condition-balanced halves for Tealeaf and every comparator. The benchmark retains the 25-modeled-gene-UMI candidate screen and requires at least four subjects for each cell-type pair; Tealeaf tests 7,671 and 5,140 candidate block-by-cell-type-pair hypotheses in the two halves. Spliced alignments supplied to the external methods are restricted to unique genomic mappings and deduplicated by cell barcode, UMI, genomic start, and CIGAR. LeafCutter tests intron clusters, MAJIQ tests edges within local splicing variations, and scQuint tests intron groups. We additionally run rMATS 4.1.2 on JCEC event counts and SUPPA2 v2.4. SUPPA2 generates the standard SE, A3SS, A5SS, MXE, and RI event definitions and transcript-TPM PSI values; its classical paired Wilcoxon statistic uses exact conditional tails in the matched split analysis, while the separately archived full-data ranking retains the earlier normal approximation. rMATS is rerun in paired mode with the PAIRADISE model on JCEC counts. The pseudobulk BAMs are single-end-like, so the count pass uses single-end read handling, while the statistical model pairs matched biological subjects; its IncLevelDifference is converted to the common level-b-minus-level-a orientation before direction checks. For each Tealeaf--comparator pair, scoring retains only genes and cell-type pairs eligible for both methods in both halves. Feature p-values are combined by Simes within each gene--cell-type pair and then across the shared pairs within a gene. For method \(m\), gene \(g\), and half \(h\in\{1,2\}\), let \(p_{mgh}\in[0,1]\) be the resulting gene p-value. The conjunction p-value \(p_{mg}^{\rm rep}=\max(p_{mg1},p_{mg2})\) tests whether the gene has signal in both halves, and BH at 0.05 is applied on the pairwise shared gene universe. Secondary endpoints are held-half nominal replication and Spearman correlation of gene-level \(-\log_{10}p\) across halves.

\section*{Results}

\subsection*{Tealeaf reproducibility against comparator methods}

Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} gives the empirically sign-flip-calibrated local-path comparison using the testing-level EB choice \(\widehat\zeta=32\). Tealeaf has more independently replicated genes than the production comparators on their matched shared universes. The matched-split SUPPA2 analysis uses the same subject-fold manifests as Tealeaf, with SUPPA2 event tests aggregated by Simes within each gene--cell-type pair. These discovery counts are empirical reproducibility results, not proof of better biological power or false-discovery control. The coverage-conditioned EC-count null assessment in the appendix identifies a depth-dependent smoothing artifact in fixed-concentration Tealeaf tests that can repeat across halves despite label/sign-flip calibration. Thus the intended information gain from combining junction, exonic, and ambiguous EC reads is not established by this table alone. The global concentration was also estimated on this benchmark dataset, so any claimed numerical advantage requires independent calibration and biological replication.

The rows in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} separate the production comparator benchmark from explicitly exploratory event-definition sensitivities. LeafCutter, MAJIQ Heterogen, scQuint, matched-split SUPPA2, and paired-JCEC rMATS use the matched shared gene--cell-type-pair split benchmark and are the direct reproducibility comparisons to Tealeaf. For SUPPA2 and rMATS, the shared unit is a gene--cell-type pair after Simes aggregation of event p-values, rather than a Tealeaf block. The paired-JCEC rMATS row uses independent statistical refits on the same two subject-half manifests and the same shared-gene conjunction procedure. On its 597-gene shared universe, Tealeaf has 155 replicated genes versus 12 for rMATS, with held-half nominal replication 0.877 versus 0.715. The separate full-data paired-JCEC results remain the source for the long-read event audit. The daggered hybrid row uses only the event-supported subset shared with direct Tealeaf and is not a production comparator. Thus the main split conclusion is based on directly matched gene--pair hypotheses, while SUPPA2 and paired rMATS provide complementary event-level sensitivity and independent long-read validation.

We also tested an exploratory hybrid that keeps SUPPA2's included-versus-excluded event definitions but replaces transcript-TPM PSI and the native event statistic with Tealeaf's primer-specific EC likelihood and paired test. The corrected hybrid profiles the event's combined inclusion-plus-exclusion abundance against other isoforms rather than holding that abundance fixed. On 1,755 shared gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses, it shows 49 replicated genes versus 71 for direct Tealeaf on the same restricted event-supported set; both results appear in the daggered row of Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}. The model construction, calibration, and limitations are described in the appendix subsection on the Tealeaf/SUPPA2 hybrid.

The fold-specific Tealeaf analyses make 1,471 and 1,047 BH calls. The pooled EB evidence rises from 0.190 at \(\zeta=2\) to 0.333 at \(\zeta=32\), then falls to 0.309 at \(\zeta=256\); fitting the halves separately selects 32 and 64. The complete selector was rerun in each of the 32 matched sign-flip null families. Every null family selected \(\zeta=2\), mean fold rejection was 0.04995 at nominal 0.05, and no fold-level null family made a BH call. Propagating each selected null family through the same within-pair Simes aggregation, across-pair gene aggregation, two-fold conjunction, and BH procedure produced zero replicated genes on every comparator-matched universe.

The 192 one-core fitting shards use 2.516 aggregate worker hours across both folds, with maximum shard elapsed time 78 seconds and peak worker resident memory 0.288 GiB. Merge-time moderation and empirical calibration add less than two minutes of serial work. This is substantially cheaper than repeatedly fitting the nested latent EC GLMM and shows that preserving primer-specific EC compatibility does not itself require the larger computational cost of the global random-effects model.

The matched-split SUPPA2 analysis uses the standard event catalogue on the production subject-fold manifests. Its event-level tests were reduced to gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses by Simes aggregation, and the resulting 2,342 hypotheses are shared with the Tealeaf direct-path results shown in the table. Correcting the signed-rank tail calculation raises replicated SUPPA2 discoveries from one to 21 genes, with 48 and 44 fold-level BH genes. Quantification, event eligibility, subject counts, the matched universe, and aggregation are held fixed. Alternative paired-$t$ statistics give 10--18 replicated genes. None of the eight statistics produces a replicated gene in any of 32 subject-synchronized sign-flip families after the complete Simes/conjunction/BH procedure. This is a finite null audit rather than a proof of family-level FDR control. The Tealeaf reference is the merged, calibrated fixed-total concentration-32 result, not a raw fitting shard or a deprecated cross-fitted smoothing run. The earlier full-data and original event-universe split outputs remain separate audits and have not been relabeled as exact-test results.

The hybrid row also uses this same selected Tealeaf procedure, giving 71 replicated direct-path genes versus 49 for the hybrid on its restricted 1,755-pair universe. The earlier apparent hybrid advantage, 49 versus 17, compared against a deprecated cross-fitted smoothing strategy and is not a comparison with current production Tealeaf. Hybrid fitting and its independent long-read results are unchanged by correcting the reference.

The primer-aware matched row uses 2,191 gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses shared with Tealeaf in both folds and 431 shared genes. The rebuilt primer-aware PSI reproduces the archived effect sizes and subject counts before changing the statistic. Exact signed-rank tails increase primer-aware SUPPA2 from zero to five replicated genes, with 13 and 16 fold-level gene BH calls, held-half nominal replication 0.861, and \(\rho=0.274\). On the same universe, the selected, merged Tealeaf fit has 173 and 166 fold-level gene BH calls, 127 replicated genes, held-half nominal replication 0.885, and \(\rho=0.811\). Paired-\(t\) and transformed-PSI alternatives yield two to four replicated genes; none of the eight statistics yields a replicated gene in 32 synchronized subject-sign null families. The matched tests and complete statistic audit are saved in \texttt{analyses/comparator\_suppa\_rmats/suppa2\_primer\_aware}.

\subsubsection*{Coverage dependence and split-effect direction}

A correlation between small p-values and high coverage can arise from power, miscalibration, or both. We therefore audit the published concentration-32 tests rather than infer validity from p-value reproducibility alone. For each matched gene and fold, coverage is the median modeled gene UMI count per pseudobulk within a cell-type pair, followed by the median across shared pairs, counting each pair once. This common EC-derived gene-coverage proxy is used for both methods; it is not a comparator-specific informative-junction count. Table~\ref{tab:coverage-direction-audit} reports Spearman correlation of the gene p-value itself with coverage, so a negative value means smaller p-values at higher depth. Tealeaf correlations range from \(-0.472\) to \(-0.297\), and remain negative after residualizing ranks on paired-subject count and number of feature tests.

The fixed-concentration sign-flip nulls also show coverage dependence. At nominal 0.05, rejection in the lowest versus highest coverage quintile is 0.0341 versus 0.0615 in fold 0, and 0.0435 versus 0.0576 in fold 1. At nominal 0.001, the highest quintile rejects at 0.00273 and 0.00235. Thus pooled calibration can conceal coverage-specific tail inflation, and the Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} advantage cannot be attributed solely to greater power on the basis of replicated p-values. These conditional diagnostics hold concentration fixed, reuse the calibration null pool, and contain correlated events; they are not an independent selector-aware FDR validation.

For directional assessment, each fold applies event BH to all eligible feature tests on the matched gene--pair universe, before intersecting event identities across folds. We select the union of events significant in either fold, not only events significant in both. Tealeaf's mean ILR effects are mapped to centered log-path effects and aligned by path signature. Two-path agreement is ordinary sign agreement; for a multi-path block it is a positive inner product of the aligned effect vectors. LeafCutter and scQuint use differences of equal-pseudobulk mean junction proportions within their tested clusters or intron groups, using only samples in that fold's contrast manifest and excluding zero-total pseudobulks from each mean. Junction components are aligned by their identities in the common count bundle. MAJIQ uses its native median PSI difference for each tested edge, without collapsing distinct edge tests into one LSV. SUPPA2 uses its paired mean PSI effect, and the hybrid uses its testing ILR effect. The archived Tealeaf pooled-baseline optimizer is used to recover directions without changing the published p-values. A recovered effect must match its published norm within \(10^{-6}\) in each fold; 267 of 7,522 and 135 of 5,055 eligible fold tests fail this check and are not assigned a direction. Nonmatching feature sets, missing effects, and zero effects are also unevaluable. Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} retains the evaluable denominators rather than assuming agreement for those exclusions.

On the SUPPA2-matched universe, 1,092 of 1,107 evaluable Tealeaf events agree, 98.6\%, among 1,171 selected common events. Equal-gene weighting gives 97.2\%, reducing domination by genes with many blocks. Agreement is 58/63 in the lowest coverage quintile and 365/367 in the highest. The two-path subset gives 774/784 sign agreements, 98.7\%, and the multi-path subset gives 318/323 positive inner products, 98.5\%. Using the original published fold-event BH thresholds instead of correcting within the matched subset gives 1,003/1,014 agreements, 98.9\%. Including all common events within genes significant in either half, rather than only event-BH discoveries, gives 1,852/2,172 agreements, 85.3\%; this is a separate weaker-signal sensitivity. High directional agreement supports consistency of the measured effects, but does not rule out a systematic artifact shared across halves or remove the coverage-specific null-calibration concern. The coverage-conditioned count nulls in the appendix show that uniform smoothing can generate cell-type differences from unequal coverage even when the true transcript mixture is identical within each subject. Thus these discovery and direction counts are empirical reproducibility measurements, not independently calibrated evidence that all of the power advantage represents biological differential splicing. Replacement testing procedures are being assessed against both endpoints and genuine count nulls. The paired rMATS split rerun replaces the historical unpaired row. Its native mean-PSI difference is converted from level-a-minus-level-b to level-b-minus-level-a before the common directional audit; rounded zero effects are unevaluable, not disagreements.

Independent EC-count null screens strengthen this concern, with approximately 29\% nominal-.05 rejection for the moderated concentration-32 paired procedure and 44\% for the concentration-32 omnibus procedure despite pooled calibration. These retain observed coverage and primer totals while generating no true cell-type path effect. The appendix evaluates replacement procedures against count nulls and both replication endpoints. The reported production advantage therefore remains descriptive reproducibility, not demonstrated controlled biological discovery power.

The contrast between the split benchmark and the long-read rank audit has both a statistical-tail component and an endpoint component. Exact paired testing repairs an extreme-tail power loss in both transcript-TPM and primer-aware SUPPA2; the separately archived full-data long-read rankings retain their earlier statistic. In the archived unrestricted first split, primer-aware SUPPA2 has 23,986 nominal \(p<0.05\) event tests and 533 \(p<10^{-3}\) tests among 331,526 tested events, but only 32 event-level BH calls. Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} does not screen on those event BH calls, it aggregates all eligible raw p-values and then corrects the replicated gene family. The long-read table deliberately ranks at most 500 nominally significant events per contrast and does not require BH discovery, so it measures directional enrichment among a selected tail rather than reproducible discovery power. Primer-aware event PSI also has a smaller median absolute effect than transcript-TPM SUPPA2 (about 0.013 versus 0.029 in the unrestricted first split). These endpoint and estimator differences do not excuse the former conservative tail calculation, and the two analyses should not be interpreted as measuring the same power.

The raw EC-conditional allocation ablation increases the median absolute split event effect from about 0.013 under the production theta allocation to about 0.027, but its fold p-values are less concentrated in the extreme tail and it produces no fold-level BH calls or replicated gene pairs. Thus the apparent theta effect shrinkage is partly an estimand difference, not evidence that raw EC allocations improve calibrated discovery. The production comparator retains theta allocation for testing; raw event allocations remain available for descriptive sensitivity analyses.

Table~\ref{tab:path-alpha-scaling} directly compares the fixed-total production scaling with the former path-dependent scaling. Set \(\alpha_{\rm path}=\zeta/2\) under the former rule, so the two penalties are identical for two-path blocks. Two-path blocks comprise 5,295 of 7,671 fold-0 candidates, while a block with \(S_b>2\) receives \(S_b\zeta/2>\zeta\) total concentration under the former rule. The curves are nearly indistinguishable around their optimum. Fixed-total scaling reaches peak fold discovery rates of 19.56\% and 21.18\%, compared with 19.55\% and 20.95\% under the path-dependent scaling. Both attain maximum replicated-gene counts of 78, 161, and 161. At \(\zeta=64\), fixed total gives 76, 161, and 161 replicated genes, versus 78, 161, and 161 at the matched \(\alpha_{\rm path}=32\); their held-half replication rates are respectively 0.897/0.907/0.903 and 0.898/0.925/0.913. At \(\zeta=128\), the rates are 0.909/0.919/0.906 under fixed total and 0.908/0.919/0.907 under matched path-dependent scaling. Pooled null rejection remains near 0.05 and no fold-level null family makes a BH call at any fixed-total setting. Fixed total concentration therefore removes an arbitrary dependence on path count without a material discovery or replication penalty, and testing-level EB selects \(\zeta=32\) rather than choosing the maximum replicated-discovery count.

The smoothing sensitivity in Table~\ref{tab:path-alpha-sensitivity} holds variance moderation, empirical calibration, gene aggregation, and the two-fold replication endpoint fixed while varying the former uniform per-path pseudocount. Without smoothing, local estimates can approach the simplex boundary and some hypotheses are lost. Replicated discovery increases through a maximum at \(\alpha_{\rm path}=32\), then decreases at 64 and again at 128 for the MAJIQ and scQuint matched universes; the LeafCutter result plateaus at 78 replicated genes at 32--64 and decreases to 77 at 128. Fold-specific block calls peak earlier, at \(\alpha_{\rm path}=16\) in half 0 and 16--32 in half 1, and also decline at higher precision. The turnover is visible in both intermediate block calls and the replication endpoint rather than being imposed by failed null calibration: every setting retains pooled sign-flip rejection near 0.05 and produces no fold-level BH calls in any of 64 null families. This grid motivates smoothing and verifies that excessive concentration eventually loses power, but it is not the selection rule; production uses fixed-total scaling and chooses \(\zeta\) from the testing-level EB objective.

\subsection*{Primary omnibus and pairwise localization}

The subject-blocked omnibus regression is the primary full-data cell-type test because it asks whether any represented cell type differs while producing one hypothesis per block. Pairwise tests are secondary, they localize which cell-type contrasts contribute to an omnibus event and provide the pair-specific hypotheses needed for comparator matching. Each family independently applies the same testing-level EB selection rather than sharing a concentration selected by the other family.


\subsection*{Full-data Tealeaf results}

The full-data omnibus cell-type, secondary pairwise cell-type, and condition-within-cell-type families each apply the testing-level EB procedure independently. All three evidence curves select fixed-total concentration \(\widehat\zeta=64\), rather than inheriting \(\widehat\zeta=32\) from the subject-split comparator benchmark. The omnibus analysis tests 1,785 blocks and the condition analysis tests 14,833 block-by-cell-type hypotheses. Table~\ref{tab:primary-local-path-results} reports their observed and design-matched null results. Of 1,781 fitted cell-type blocks, the omnibus test calls 791 blocks from 572 genes. The condition family has nominal enrichment but no BH event.

\begin{tabular}{lrrrrrrrr}

The full cell-type result is consistent with the subject-split comparator benchmark rather than resting only on a large full-data discovery count. The two analyses estimate their concentrations independently, selecting 64 in full data and 32 in the split benchmark, and Tealeaf exceeds all three comparators in independently replicated genes on their matched universes. The complete null conjunction pipelines produce no replicated gene. The condition result shows that empirical calibration is not mechanically producing discoveries, its 1,243 nominal events do not reach the calibrated BH tail.

From prepared inputs, the selected omnibus, secondary pairwise, and condition fits use 0.488, 1.240, and 1.670 aggregate one-core worker hours, with peak worker memory of 0.500, 0.396, and 0.399 GiB. Fitting and calibrating all eight candidates costs 5.020, 11.211, and 12.801 worker hours. Selection therefore multiplies the one-concentration fitting cost, although the grid is embarrassingly parallel and is estimated once for the complete analysis family. The condition implementation reuses the REML biological variance fitted under its intercept-only null across label permutations, because that null is unchanged by the permutation.

The full selector is rerun independently inside each of the 32 matched null families. Selector-aware mean rejection at nominal 0.05 is 0.05043 for omnibus, 0.05000 for pairwise, and 0.04995 for condition, and no selected null family makes a BH call. All 32 pairwise null families select \(\zeta=2\); the omnibus and condition null selectors choose a range of values, so calibration audits the selection rule rather than only the observed \(\zeta=64\) fit. The older 64- and 128-replicate null-budget sensitivity used fixed add-two smoothing and is retained in the appendix as a calibration-budget check, not as a rerun of the EB selector.

\subsection*{Independent long-read replication}

The independent long-read data can evaluate 1,401 of the 2,950 secondary pairwise Tealeaf discoveries from matched cell types. All tested paths map to source annotated transcripts for 1,338 tests from 369 genes, and 704 tests pass the primary pooled-depth rule. Their pooled long-read effects agree with Tealeaf for 405 of 704 tests, a 57.5\% directional replication rate with 95\% Wilson interval 53.8--61.1\%, compared with the 50\% sign-flip null (\(p=3.68\times10^{-5}\)). Requiring a Tealeaf path-effect norm of at least 0.05 retains 403 tests and raises pooled replication to 59.8\%, interval 54.9--64.5\% (\(p=4.87\times10^{-5}\)).

The stricter replicate-resolved rule retains 459 tests, of which 175 agree with Tealeaf in both long-read replicates, 38.1\% with interval 33.8--42.7\%. The conditional sign-flip null is 33.1\%, and the orientation test gives \(p=0.00487\). At minimum Tealeaf effect norm 0.05, 119 of 276 tests agree in both replicates, 43.1\% with interval 37.4--49.0\%, compared with a 35.1\% conditional null (\(p=0.000972\)). Selecting only the largest eligible effect per gene gives similar rates, 57.1\% pooled and 38.8\% strict without an effect threshold, so repeated contrasts within genes do not explain the aggregate result. Excluding cross-region astrocyte and oligodendrocyte mappings gives 54.6\% pooled replication among 480 tests and 34.3\% strict replication among 309 tests, still above their conditional nulls. Expanding oligodendrocytes from mature MFOL/MOL annotations to every annotated stage changes either primary rate by less than 0.6 percentage points.

A pooled long-read UMI G-test passes BH and has the Tealeaf direction for 228 of 704 tests, 32.4\%. This is a secondary read-level endpoint: the two source animals per region do not support a well-powered biological-replicate significance test for each event. The directional endpoints therefore measure whether independently generated long reads support the sign of selected Tealeaf effects, not a second genome-wide discovery analysis. Figure~\ref{fig:tilgner-long-read-replication} shows the coverage and effect-size sensitivities.

\centering
\includegraphics[width=.82\linewidth]{../analyses/tilgner_long_read_replication/long_read_replication.pdf}
\caption{Directional replication of BH-significant secondary Tealeaf cell-type contrasts in independent P56 mouse-brain long reads from Joglekar et al. Tealeaf significance uses \(\zeta_{\rm test}=64\), while displayed effect-size thresholds use the \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) refit.}
\label{fig:tilgner-long-read-replication}
\end{figure}

Junction methods differ sharply in conditional external replication, Table~\ref{tab:tilgner-method-replication}. LeafCutter and scQuint have higher pooled rates than Tealeaf, 87.5\% and 83.7\% versus 57.5\%, and the advantage persists under the two-replicate endpoint. Relaxed-coverage MAJIQ is directionally consistent for 86 of 139 eligible LSVs, 61.9\%, and 54 of 113 LSVs in both source replicates, 47.8\%. The paired junction-CLR ablation is close to Tealeaf, 59.5\% pooled and 39.8\% in both replicates versus 57.5\% and 38.1\%. The event baselines are less concordant: full-data SUPPA2 agrees for 1,872 of 3,871 pooled calls, 48.4\%, and for 802 of 2,590 calls in both source replicates, 31.0\%; corrected primer-aware SUPPA2 agrees for 1,782 of 3,873 pooled calls, 46.0\%, and for 812 of 2,626 calls in both source replicates, 30.9\%; paired-JCEC rMATS agrees for 1,778 of 3,859 pooled calls, 46.1\%, and for 660 of 2,549 strict calls, 25.9\%. The event rows use the top 500 ranked events per contrast rather than the BH-selected block or LSV universes and are therefore an event-level audit, not a matched power comparison. Retaining only the largest effect per gene--cell-type contrast changes the pooled rates to 87.3\%, 62.4\%, 59.2\%, 56.2\%, and 83.3\% for LeafCutter, MAJIQ, paired CLR, Tealeaf, and scQuint, respectively, so repeated calls do not explain the junction-method ordering.

These are conditional validation rates rather than a matched power comparison. Complete source representation and the depth rule retain 704 Tealeaf calls, 139 MAJIQ LSVs, 56 LeafCutter clusters, and 43 scQuint groups. The event audit retains 3,871 full-data SUPPA2 events, 3,873 corrected primer-aware SUPPA2 events, and 3,859 paired-JCEC rMATS events after the top-500-per-contrast and depth filters. The relaxed MAJIQ build tests 10,297 edges in the 28 mapped cell-type contrasts and calls 2,657 edges from 466 genes; restoring significant edges to multijunction LSVs, mapping every junction to the common short-read bundle, requiring complete source-transcript representation, and applying the long-read depth rule produces the 139 evaluable LSVs. The eligible LeafCutter and scQuint sets are enriched for large, clean junction-composition shifts, with median short-read effect norms 0.190 and 0.619 compared with median path-composition norm 0.062 for Tealeaf; MAJIQ's median common-estimator junction norm is 0.017, although the coordinates differ and these magnitudes are not directly interchangeable. Thus the external data do not support claiming that each selected Tealeaf call replicates better than junction-method calls. They instead show broader evaluable Tealeaf coverage, similar conditional replication to the paired CLR ablation, higher conditional replication for each junction method, and somewhat lower directional agreement for the event baselines. The subject-split benchmark remains the matched comparison of discovery power because it uses a shared gene--contrast universe in both halves.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=.82\linewidth]{../analyses/tilgner_junction_replication/sign_agreement_by_rank.pdf}
\caption{Cumulative directional agreement with independent long-read effects as a function of method-specific significance rank. The plot shows up to 200 long-read-evaluable calls per method, ordered by calibrated p-value, then continuous raw p-value and the corresponding test's statistic within ties. LeafCutter, MAJIQ, and scQuint are ranked from all converged finite tests, without an FDR cutoff; their nonsignificant tests are eligible. Source eligibility is applied before truncating to 200. Calls require complete source representation, at least 20 pooled long-read UMIs per cell type, and a finite pooled direction; the x-axis is integer rank rather than a method-specific fraction. Tealeaf omnibus points use the strongest source-evaluable production pairwise direction per significant block, ranked from the complete EB-selected omnibus table with its own raw tail and statistic. Tealeaf pairwise, paired CLR, and the isoform-ratio audit retain their discovery-selected inputs; the latter has only ten mapped calls. Full-data SUPPA2, primer-aware SUPPA2, paired-JCEC rMATS, and the hybrid are included as event-level rank audits. The horizontal dashed line is the 50\% orientation null.}
\label{fig:tilgner-sign-agreement-rank}
\end{figure}

We summarize the rank curve by its normalized discrete area. For integer cutoff \(K\geq1\), let \(a_j\in\{0,1\}\) indicate positive directional agreement at rank \(j\), and let \(c_k=k^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^k a_j\) be cumulative agreement through rank \(k\). The summary is \(A_K=K^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^K c_k\), which gives greater weight to early agreements and has expectation 0.5 under randomized orientation. It is not ROC AUC. We report \(A_{100}\) with the equal-weight top-100 agreement \(c_{100}\), Table~\ref{tab:lr-rank-summary}. No value is extrapolated for a method with fewer than 100 evaluable calls. LeafCutter, scQuint, and MAJIQ now have 372, 1,830, and 385 all-tested source-evaluable associations, respectively, and each contributes 200 plotted ranks. Their original significance-selected replication summaries remain separate. These conditional, method-specific universes do not make the rank summary a matched discovery-power comparison.

The Tealeaf/SUPPA2 hybrid curve in Figure~\ref{fig:tilgner-sign-agreement-rank} is ranked from its complete set of 7,182 converged event--contrast tests. Direct mapping to the source transcript counts represents both event classes and both source replicates for 2,758 tests; 1,194 also pass the pooled 20-UMI and finite-effect rules. The curve ranks those eligible tests by the hybrid's calibrated p-value, moderated analytic tail, and test statistic, without a SUPPA2 significance or overlap screen. The corrected fit profiles event mass, and reports a mean paired PSI difference from a separate refit with total reporting pseudocount 1, while retaining ILR testing at \(\zeta=64\). Positive sign agreement is 41/75 (54.7\%) among its first 75 tests and 104/200 (52.0\%) among its first 200, versus 92/200 (46.0\%) for the former fixed-mass fit with its strongly smoothed testing effect. Across all eligible corrected hybrid tests, pooled agreement is 560/1,194 (46.9\%). This remains below full-data SUPPA2's 71\% top-200 agreement on its own evaluable universe. However, among the 658 evaluable hypotheses shared by both methods, each ranked by its own significance, SUPPA2 gives 112/200 (56.0\%) and the corrected hybrid gives 113/200 (56.5\%); the former hybrid gave 96/200 (48.0\%). The saved SUPPA2 comparison contains five internal-event categories, whereas the expanded hybrid also tests alternative first and last exons; EC-model completeness and coverage further restrict its event set. Thus the raw cross-method gap combines an event-universe difference with estimation and ranking differences, rather than establishing that the hybrid is uniformly worse on the same events. These matched-universe results are sensitivity diagnostics, not a replacement for the complete rank curves.

The tie-aware curve changes the Tealeaf pairwise top-75 rate from 57.3\% to 58.7\%. The pairwise curve remains close to its full eligible rate of 57.5\% (405 of 704), so the original top-75 deficit was not only a plotting artifact. The calibrated empirical-null p-values have only 21 unique values among the top 200 Tealeaf calls; 69 of the first 75 share one p-value. The continuous tie-break makes the ordering reproducible, but the rank curve should not be interpreted as a finely resolved Tealeaf significance ranking. The omnibus curve additionally corrects an earlier cached input that truncated the production table before source eligibility and used the selected pairwise statistic to break omnibus ties. It now uses the complete EB-selected omnibus table and its own continuous tail; the earlier omnibus rank summary is superseded, not a change to its statistical fits. We also remapped the complete union of all eight fixed-total discovery sets to the independent source transcript matrix, rather than reusing the \(\zeta=64\) mapping intersection. Fixed-total concentrations give 65.3\%, 68.0\%, 65.3\%, 61.3\%, 56.0\%, 58.7\%, 60.0\%, and 60.0\% top-75 agreement after full remapping for \(\zeta=2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256\), respectively. The corresponding pairwise discovery sets contain 1,594, 2,152, 2,575, 2,825, 2,922, 2,950, 2,919, and 2,871 calls, with 351, 491, 594, 661, 696, 704, 690, and 677 calls passing the common source-mapping and depth filters. The full results are recorded in \texttt{zeta\_sensitivity.tsv} and \texttt{zeta\_full\_remap.tsv}.

We also ranked the three event baselines against the same independent source, using the top 500 tests per cell-type contrast to keep the event-unit comparison computationally bounded and then plotting the top 200 evaluable associations. The final rank table contains 142 of 200 positive agreements for full-data SUPPA2 (71.0\%), 128 of 200 for corrected primer-aware SUPPA2 (64.0\%), and 105 of 200 for paired-JCEC rMATS (52.5\%). Their top-500-per-contrast audits give 1,872 of 3,871, 1,782 of 3,873, and 1,778 of 3,859 pooled agreements, respectively, with 802 of 2,590, 812 of 2,626, and 660 of 2,549 calls agreeing in both source replicates. The higher top-rank agreement is not a genome-wide power claim because the original event-universe split audits are not matched to Tealeaf; the matched-split gene--pair aggregation in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} is a separate analysis. These event-level results are not substituted for the full-data Tealeaf table above: they use event coordinates and event-specific PSI estimators, while the paired-JCEC rMATS values use paired PAIRADISE statistics and the common post-selection direction estimator.

The earlier unpaired rMATS result was nearly uninformative: 1,229,586 of 1,229,628 event p-values were exactly one, and no event survived BH. The corrected full-data paired-JCEC rMATS rerun tests 5,864,730 event--contrast hypotheses across 190 cell-type contrasts; 1,987,601 p-values are below one and 1,497 events pass the within-contrast JCEC FDR threshold. The FDR calls comprise 711 SE, 621 MXE, 102 A3SS, 44 A5SS, and 19 RI events. Thus the paired model materially restores a usable event-level test distribution, although its event multiplicity remains much larger than Tealeaf's block hypotheses. The corrected full-data SUPPA2 event audit contributes 653,199 cell-type event--contrast tests over 9,064 events and 1,312 event-level BH calls, while the matched-split gene--cell-type-pair aggregation in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} yields five replicated genes for primer-aware SUPPA2 with exact paired testing. Full-data SUPPA2 gives 48.4\% pooled and 31.0\% strict long-read directional replication. Corrected primer-aware SUPPA2 gives 46.0\% pooled and 30.9\% strict long-read directional replication; its lower rate is consistent with a different event PSI estimator and is not a matched power claim. Together with the Tealeaf, LeafCutter, MAJIQ, and scQuint rows above, these results give both a matched split reproducibility comparison and a broader event-level audit. The split reproducibility row for rMATS uses independent paired-JCEC refits and a directly matched conjunction analysis, rather than reusing full-data p-values.

We also added an isoform-usage-ratio baseline. It fits the same subject-paired local compositions with a path-ratio likelihood, without the EC likelihood used by Tealeaf. After pooling the eight 50-permutation shards and applying the pooled empirical null, 89 pairwise calls pass FDR 0.05. Only ten of those calls overlap the current independent long-read mapping table, so their observed 40\% directional agreement is descriptive and is not a fair replacement for the full comparator benchmark. A complete comparison requires remapping all ratio-baseline calls to the source transcript matrix.

Biological summaries use the 791 BH-significant blocks from the primary omnibus test, not the secondary pairwise localization family. Table~\ref{tab:production-event-types} classifies each omnibus-significant block once from the supported paths in its fitted partition. A block adjacent to a terminal pseudo-anchor is called an alternative first or last \emph{region}, because the paths may differ at several exons or transcript ends and do not necessarily isolate one promoter, terminal exon, or polyadenylation choice. Transcript-end regions dominate, but 139 distinct cassette-exon blocks are also discovered. The secondary pairwise analysis independently selects \(\zeta=64\), makes 2,950 calls across 716 distinct blocks, and is used only to localize which cell-type contrasts may drive an omnibus discovery and to match comparator hypotheses.

Eight of nine literature-audited functional splice events pass BH in the primary omnibus family. The \emph{Ntrk2} alternative-last-region block jointly differs across eight represented cell types and separates short kinase-deficient from long kinase-containing TrkB paths, including the TrkB.T1 family; astrocytes predominantly express TrkB.T1, and its loss impairs astrocyte morphogenesis and support of excitatory synaptogenesis. The \emph{Grin1} block jointly differs across five cell types and isolates the 111-nt C1 cassette, whose retention motifs alter GluN1 surface trafficking. The \emph{App} exon-15 cassette jointly differs across nine cell types; exclusion produces L-APP and changes polarized secretion and amyloidogenic processing. The six-path \emph{Dlg4} alternative-first-region block jointly differs across six cell types and overlaps the functional PSD-95 alpha/beta isoform system, but the fitted partition does not isolate that pair of starts. Omnibus FDR is 0.000959 for \emph{Ntrk2}, \emph{App}, and \emph{Dlg4}, and 0.00205 for \emph{Grin1}.

Screening the remaining omnibus catalog identified four additional exact structural matches. The \emph{Gria2} flip/flop and \emph{Scn2a} 5N/5A pairs differ across eight and five represented cell types and each has FDR 0.000959; the former changes AMPA-receptor desensitization and steady-state current, while the latter alters Nav1.2 activation and the effects of disease variants. The 93-nt \emph{Rbfox3} RRM cassette differs across four cell types and has FDR 0.00206; its exclusion disrupts Rbfox3 stability and splicing activity in the characterized systems. The 21-nt \emph{Mef2d} beta microexon differs across four cell types and has FDR 0.0146; beta inclusion strengthens transcriptional activation in reporter assays. The audited \emph{Enah} exon-11a block does not pass omnibus BH. These literature matches establish experimentally characterized consequences for the same local structures, not the direction, causal role, or mechanism of every observed cell-type contrast.

The complete omnibus discovery catalog, component annotations, coordinates, empirical p-values, FDR values, and literature-audit intersection are recorded in the repository's local-path omnibus event-annotation directory. The separate local-path full-event annotation directory retains the secondary pairwise localization catalog.

\clearpage
\subsection*{Primer-separated read support}

We first checked all significant omnibus discoveries against alignment-level evidence rather than selecting favorable examples. Of 791 BH-significant blocks, 750 have at least one explicit endpoint exon that can serve as a local depth reference; the remaining 41 are whole-gene complexes without a local endpoint anchor. Every aligned read was required to map uniquely and was deduplicated by cell barcode, UMI, genomic start, and CIGAR. Alternative donor and acceptor exons were split at every tested path boundary so that only their discriminating atomic segments contributed. For feature \(f\) in block \(b\), cell type \(c\), and primer \(p\), let \(\widehat u_{fc}\in[0,1]\) be the mean \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) fitted proportion of paths containing \(f\), let \(s_{fcp}\in\mathbb R_+\) be pooled aligned depth for an exon segment or pooled UMI count for a junction, and let \(a_{bcp}\in\mathbb R_+\) be mean aligned depth over the block's one or two explicit endpoint exons. The raw endpoint-normalized evidence is \(r_{fcp}=s_{fcp}/a_{bcp}\). This normalization asks whether a feature changes relative to local transcript abundance and prevents cell number or gene-expression differences from setting the comparison. Because exon depth and junction count remain on different scales and primer design changes their proportionality to path abundance, we compare cell-type ranks within each feature rather than their absolute magnitudes.

For each feature and primer we pooled signal and endpoint depth across subjects, retained at least three cell types with pooled endpoint depth at least 20 and fitted range at least 0.05, and computed the Spearman correlation between \(\widehat u_{fc}\) and \(r_{fcp}\) across cell types. Feature correlations were averaged within a block so that a block with many discriminating features did not dominate. Figure~\ref{fig:path-coverage-concordance} shows positive median block-level agreement in every feature--primer stratum. For exon segments the median correlations are 0.18, 95\% bootstrap interval 0.03--0.30, across 115 poly(dT) blocks and 0.12, interval 0.06--0.25, across 288 random-hexamer blocks. For junctions they are 0.27, interval 0.18--0.39, across 76 poly(dT) blocks and 0.26, interval 0.15--0.33, across 190 random-hexamer blocks. The fractions of blocks with positive agreement are 61\%, 57\%, 71\%, and 66\%, respectively, with one-sided sign-test p-values 0.0124, 0.00777, 0.000156, and \(4.04\times10^{-6}\). Junction agreement is stronger, while the positive exon result shows that exonic observations also track the fitted changes. This is a descriptive same-read check, not independent replication or evidence used for statistical calibration, but it establishes that significant fitted changes usually order cell types in the direction visible in local raw coverage.

\begin{figure}[p]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{../analyses/path_coverage_concordance/path_coverage_rank_concordance.pdf}
\caption{Agreement between weakly regularized fitted path usage and endpoint-normalized alignment evidence across significant omnibus blocks. Each point is one support-qualified block and averages within-feature Spearman correlations across cell types. Boxes show medians and interquartile ranges, annotations give block counts, bootstrap intervals for the median, and bootstrap intervals for the positive fraction, and the horizontal line marks no directional agreement. Significance is inherited from the production \(\zeta_{\rm test}=64\) omnibus analysis; fitted inclusion uses the separate \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) reporting refit and cannot alter discovery.}
\label{fig:path-coverage-concordance}
\end{figure}

Figures~\ref{fig:app-sashimi}--\ref{fig:grin1-sashimi} provide detailed alignment-level views of three strong, high-confidence omnibus discoveries. For each block, we first restrict to secondary pairwise contrasts with \(\zeta_{\rm test}=64\) BH FDR below 0.05 and display the pair that maximizes the \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) effect norm $\lVert\bar{\boldsymbol\Delta}\rVert_2$, where $\boldsymbol\Delta\in\mathbb R^{S-1}$ is a subject-specific paired difference in the $S-1$ local-path isometric log-ratio coordinates and $S\in\mathbb N$ is the number of tested paths. The selected reporting-fit effect norms are 1.152 for cortical excitatory versus hippocampal pyramidal cells in \emph{App}, 0.294 for cortical excitatory versus midbrain inhibitory cells in \emph{Gria2}, and 1.498 for medium-spiny versus midbrain-inhibitory cells in \emph{Grin1}. Each track pools unique-mapping, UMI-deduplicated spliced alignments within one cell type and priming strategy, then normalizes coverage and junction support per 1,000 primer-specific half-cells. Arc labels use the same units. The transcript models below each panel are the tested local paths, and long introns are compressed for display.

Both priming strategies support the annotated alternatives, while their absolute coverage profiles differ. This is expected because poly(dT) and random-hexamer priming have different positional exposure and is why Tealeaf retains separate primer likelihoods. Below each sashimi panel, grouped bars show the \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) omnibus-refit mean proportion for each tested path across paired subjects, with standard-error bars. Using the omnibus reporting refit gives each subject--cell-type combination one estimate independent of which pair is displayed. These usage estimates jointly use both primer-specific likelihoods and all compatible observations, so they are indexed by cell type and path rather than priming strategy; Path 1 and Path 2 follow the transcript-model order shown below the read tracks. The read tracks are descriptive checks rather than a reconstruction of the test because they use spliced genomic alignments only. The primary omnibus test also uses exonic, unspliced, and ambiguous EC observations through primer-specific compatibility maps and includes every represented cell type.

Track labels and normalization follow Figure~\ref{fig:app-sashimi}; the lower panel shows \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) mean path proportions with standard errors across nine paired subjects.}
\label{fig:grin1-sashimi}
\end{figure}

The plotted intervals contain 2,210 poly(dT) and 5,122 random-hexamer spliced UMIs from cortical excitatory cells and 186 and 478 from hippocampal pyramidal cells for \emph{App}. Corresponding counts are 1,039/904 for cortical-excitatory and 167/189 for midbrain-inhibitory cells in \emph{Gria2}, and 555/384 medium-spiny plus 148/330 midbrain-inhibitory UMIs for \emph{Grin1}. These raw totals establish that the visible structures do not arise from a few alignments; normalization in the figures prevents unequal half-cell numbers from setting track scale.

We searched all 791 significant omnibus blocks for isoform switches. Hypothesis tests, calibrated p-values, and discovery selection retain the testing-level empirical-Bayes concentration \(\zeta_{\rm test}=64\). Because that concentration is optimized for calibrated discovery rather than unbiased effect estimation, reported effect sizes and path usage are refitted after selection with the weak fixed-total concentration \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\). For each tested path, we computed the range of this weakly regularized mean usage across cell types and asked whether the identity of the most-used path changed. Ninety blocks change dominant path, but none contains a path whose cell-type means span from at most 0.1 to at least 0.9. Figures~\ref{fig:agtpbp1-switch}--\ref{fig:dclk1-switch} therefore show the three largest unique-gene, non-whole-gene dominant-path changes rather than describing them as complete 0-to-1 switches. The largest path ranges are 0.344--0.965 for \emph{Agtpbp1} path 2, 0.258--0.836 for \emph{Dlg4} path 3, and 0.171--0.739 for \emph{Dclk1} path 4; all three omnibus FDR values are 0.000959.

The reporting concentration is a transparent stability convention and does not enter testing. All of \(\zeta\in\{0.5,1,2\}\) retain 1,781 of 1,785 candidate fits. Across matched subject--cell-type--path estimates, \(\zeta=1\) versus \(\zeta=0.5\) has Pearson correlation 0.997 and mean absolute difference 0.021, while \(\zeta=1\) versus \(\zeta=2\) has correlation 0.995 and mean absolute difference 0.027. Among production-significant blocks, the individual path-usage 1st, 5th, 95th, and 99th percentiles are 0.003, 0.010, 0.959, and 0.986 at \(\zeta=1\), compared with 0.070, 0.129, 0.624, and 0.734 at \(\zeta=64\). Removing smoothing yields three apparent near-complete cell-type mean switches, but loses 99 candidate fits and puts the corresponding tail percentiles at zero and one. Two of those three apparent switches have their low-usage extreme supported by only one subject. Thus \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) removes most testing-prior compression without adopting an unstable boundary estimator. The absence of a complete switch remains unresolved rather than a biological negative result.

Each figure contains separate poly(dT) and random-hexamer matrices and every cell type represented in that block's omnibus fit. Columns begin with a \(\zeta_{\rm report}=1\) mean path proportion and then follow that path's variable exons and junctions in 5$'$-to-3$'$ RNA order. Exons and junctions present in every displayed path are omitted because they cannot distinguish path usage. Shared nonconstitutive features are repeated but keep gene-global E and J labels. Weakly regularized path usage and empirical exon and junction PSI share one fixed 0--1 color scale, and path columns retain percentage labels. For exon \(e\), cell type \(c\), and primer \(p\), let \(d_{ecp}\in\mathbb R_+\) be pooled mean aligned depth and let \(F_e\) contain the nearest path-shared exon on each available side. Variable-exon PSI is \(\widehat{\operatorname{PSI}}^{\rm exon}_{ecp}=\min\{1,d_{ecp}/(|F_e|^{-1}\sum_{f\in F_e}d_{fcp})\}\); a zero anchor denominator is shown as missing. The path-shared exons used as depth references are not displayed. This is a local depth-ratio estimate, not an additional fitted model. For junction \(j\), let \(c_{jcp}\in\mathbb N_0\) be its pooled UMI count, \(d_j\) and \(a_j\) its donor and acceptor, and \(\mathcal J_j=\{h:d_h=d_j\text{ or }a_h=a_j\}\) the set of all observed junctions sharing either splice site, including junctions whose other endpoint lies outside the displayed block. Junction PSI is \(\widehat{\operatorname{PSI}}^{\rm junction}_{jcp}=c_{jcp}/\sum_{h\in\mathcal J_j}c_{hcp}\); a zero denominator is missing. Thus absolute depth and unequal half-cell counts do not determine the PSI colors, while the two primer panels can reveal protocol-specific support.

\section{Deprecated Full-Data EC GLMM}

Let \(r_g=T_g-1\in\mathbb N\) be the number of free reference-logit coordinates and let \(\bm\eta_{gi}=(\bm\eta_{gi,-}^{\mathsf T},0)^{\mathsf T}\in\mathbb R^{T_g}\) append a zero reference-isoform logit. The primer-specific EC probability vector \(\bm q_{gip}(\bm\eta_{gi})\in\Delta^{K_{gp}-1}\) has components \[q_{gipk}(\bm\eta_{gi})=\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{T_g}A_{gpkt}\exp(\eta_{git})}{\sum_{h=1}^{K_{gp}}\sum_{t=1}^{T_g}A_{gpht}\exp(\eta_{git})},\qquad k\in\{1,\ldots,K_{gp}\}.\] The deprecated analysis fitted a latent global-isoform EC GLMM separately for every block. Let \(m(i)\in\{1,\ldots,M_b\}\) identify pseudobulk \(i\)'s mouse. Its multinomial form was \(\bm c_{gip}\mid\bm\eta_{gi}\sim\operatorname{Multinomial}\{n_{gip},\bm q_{gip}(\bm\eta_{gi})\}\), with \(\bm\eta_{gi,-}=\mathcal F_{bi}(\bm\beta_b)+\bm u_{g,m(i)}\) and \(\bm u_{gm}\sim N(\bm0_{r_g},\sigma_g^2I_{r_g})\). The logistic-normal extension added \(\bm e_{gi}\sim N(\bm0_{r_g},\tau_g^2I_{r_g})\) to each pseudobulk. Both primer likelihoods shared the same latent composition. The tested block coefficients used the pooled-baseline path-ILR Jacobian \(J_b\in\mathbb R^{d_b\times r_g}\), its right inverse \(H_b=J_b^\prime(J_bJ_b^\prime)^{-1}\in\mathbb R^{r_g\times d_b}\), and a nuisance basis \(N_b\in\mathbb R^{r_g\times(r_g-d_b)}\) for \(\ker(J_b)\). This gave a corrected tangent null while retaining global isoform nuisance directions.

\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Latex,node distance=5mm and 7mm,every node/.style={font=\small},stage/.style={rounded corners=2pt,draw=tealeafNavy!60,line width=.7pt,minimum height=15mm,align=center,inner sep=5pt},flow/.style={->,line width=1.1pt,tealeafTeal},branch/.style={->,line width=1pt,tealeafOrange}]

\node[stage,fill=tealeafLight,text width=32mm,above right=3mm and 9mm of iso] (dtec) {\textbf{oligo(dT) EC proportions}\\$A_{g,\mathrm{dT}}\in\mathbb R_+^{K_{g,\mathrm{dT}}\times T_g}$\\$\bm q_{gi,\mathrm{dT}}\in\Delta^{K_{g,\mathrm{dT}}-1}$};
\node[stage,fill=tealeafLight,text width=32mm,below right=3mm and 9mm of iso] (rhec) {\textbf{random-hexamer EC proportions}\\$A_{g,\mathrm{RH}}\in\mathbb R_+^{K_{g,\mathrm{RH}}\times T_g}$\\$\bm q_{gi,\mathrm{RH}}\in\Delta^{K_{g,\mathrm{RH}}-1}$};
\node[stage,fill=tealeafLightTeal,text width=29mm,right=of dtec] (dtcount) {\textbf{Observed EC counts}\\$\bm c_{gi,\mathrm{dT}}\sim\operatorname{Multinomial}$\\$(n_{gi,\mathrm{dT}},\bm q_{gi,\mathrm{dT}})$};
\node[stage,fill=tealeafLightTeal,text width=29mm,right=of rhec] (rhcount) {\textbf{Observed EC counts}\\$\bm c_{gi,\mathrm{RH}}\sim\operatorname{Multinomial}$\\$(n_{gi,\mathrm{RH}},\bm q_{gi,\mathrm{RH}})$};

\caption{Generative flow for the deprecated global EC GLMM for gene $g=g(b)$ and pseudobulk $i$. The two primer maps share the same latent global isoform composition.}
\label{fig:deprecated-ec-generative-model}
\end{figure}

Two fitting options remain implemented. Full-covariance tilted variational inference factorizes across mice but retains unrestricted covariance within each mouse's latent vector; it uses an analytic tilted log-sum-exp bound and exact Gaussian KL term. Laplace fitting instead integrates around each mouse-specific posterior mode and uses implicit differentiation with analytic EC likelihood derivatives. The corrected full-data analysis calibrated nested variational objective gains using parametric null simulation, a cross-fitted generalized Pareto tail, and a leave-block-out empirical null CDF. GPD means generalized Pareto distribution. The corresponding derivations, algorithms, and computational comparisons are retained in the supporting sections below.

The corrected GLMM tested 4,825 cell-type partitions and 14,833 block-by-cell-type condition hypotheses. Both calibrated null families matched the audited nominal rates, but neither observed family contained a BH discovery.
