\section{Exploratory and Supporting Analyses}

\subsection*{Long-read ranking and reporting sensitivities}

The exploratory rank audit keeps the production EB-selected p-values, BH discovery sets, and matched-split comparison unchanged, while separating the ordering statistic from the reporting estimator. Starting from 704 source-evaluable production pairwise discoveries, we compare the existing equal-subject mean path-proportion difference at total reporting concentration 1 with pooled local-path estimates, primer-balanced pooled local-path estimates, primer-specific estimates, and unrestricted pooled isoform estimates. Pooled estimation sums EC counts within each cell type separately for each primer before fitting the same local-path likelihood. Primer balancing rescales each positive primer count vector to the mean of the two primer totals within that cell type; it is a reporting sensitivity, not an alternative biological replication model. Both local estimators retain total reporting concentration 1. Failed or zero-direction reporting fits retain the original direction as an explicit fallback rather than being dropped to improve agreement.

With the original calibrated-p-value-first ranking, pairwise Tealeaf has \(A_{100}=65.6\%\) and 58\% top-100 agreement. Ranking the same discoveries by the continuous analytic tail gives 73.0\% and 65\%, without changing any testing p-value. Combining that ranking with pooled local reporting gives 79.4\% and 67\%; balancing primer totals gives 80.0\% and 67\%. Unrestricted pooled isoform reporting reaches 75.8\% under the continuous ranking, rather than explaining the improvement through unconstrained global isoform fitting. Neither ranking by reporting effect magnitude, multiplying that magnitude by log evidence, nor prioritizing cross-primer concordance improves on the 80.0\% result. Thus these changes narrow but do not close the gap to full-data SUPPA2's 90.7\% \(A_{100}\) on its own evaluable universe. They were assessed using the long-read outcomes and remain exploratory; they are not promoted as independently validated improvements or as a claim of higher matched power. Preserving Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} here follows from leaving its p-values and testing-level effects unchanged, not from validating a new statistical test.

The source matrix contains fractional transcript-count entries. Saved source path-count vectors now retain that precision instead of converting entries to integers. Reconstructing the 2,950 published pairwise records from the source matrix verifies unchanged long-read effect norms and depth values; the original agreement calculation already used full-precision counts. The correction is needed when recomputing agreement for a new reporting estimator from the saved arrays. Full reporting effects and ranking sensitivities are retained in \path{analyses/tilgner_long_read_replication/ranking_audit}.

\subsubsection*{Coverage-conditioned EC-count nulls}

To distinguish biological replication from a coverage-related effect repeated across subject halves, we simulate EC counts under an actual biological null, rather than treating permuted fitted labels as the null truth. Let \(\boldsymbol\theta_u\in\mathbb R^T\) be a subject's transcript simplex, \(\mathbf C_r\in\mathbb R^{E_r\times T}\) its primer-specific compatibility matrix, and \(N_{ucr}\) the observed gene-count total for subject \(u\), cell type \(c\), and primer \(r\), rounded to an integer. Every cell type of subject \(u\) has the identical true mixture \(\boldsymbol\theta_u\). Counts are drawn as \begin{equation}\mathbf n_{ucr}\sim\operatorname{Multinomial}\left(N_{ucr},\frac{\mathbf C_r\boldsymbol\theta_u}{\mathbf1^\top\mathbf C_r\boldsymbol\theta_u}\right).\end{equation} Subject mixtures are generated by adding independent Gaussian log-transcript offsets with standard deviation .5 to the pooled mixture; a separate control sets this standard deviation to zero. Observed coverage, primer totals, compatibility, and missing observations are retained. Both the gene baseline and the path fits are re-estimated on each count draw. A fixed random sample of 128 tested hypotheses, selected without a significance screen, supplies 16 count draws per hypothesis and 32 permutation or sign-flip families per draw for calibration, excluding each hypothesis's own null values. This conditional assessment fixes the tested concentration rather than reselecting EB strength, and does not preserve a coherent joint-gene resampling distribution.

Uniform smoothing has a simple failure mode even without EC ambiguity. For direct path counts at true usage \(\pi_{us}\), total concentration \(A\), and \(S\) paths, the estimator \(\widehat\psi_{ucs}=(x_{ucs}+A/S)/(N_{uc}+A)\) has conditional expectation \((N_{uc}\pi_{us}+A/S)/(N_{uc}+A)\). Two cell types with the same \(\pi_{us}\) but different \(N_{uc}\) therefore have different expected estimated usage unless \(\pi_{us}=1/S\). EC ambiguity changes the information available per count but does not remove this mechanism. Log-ratio transformations introduce additional sampling-bias differences. Repeated cell-type coverage patterns can reproduce the artifact in both subject halves, while an external count-based usage difference need not agree. Label permutations and paired sign flips do not repair a fitted contrast whose biological-null mean is already nonzero.

At nominal .05, the existing uniform concentration-32 omnibus statistic rejects 881 of 2,000 count nulls, 44.1\%, after permutation calibration. Freeing all within-path and outside-block transcript shares gives 44.6\%, and removing subject mixture heterogeneity gives 45.6\%. Thus neither fixed transcript shares nor subject heterogeneity explains the dominant failure in this simulation. Baseline-centered concentration-32 quantification gives 4.7\% rejection on its 1,834 successfully assessed draws; its different convergence denominator is retained rather than interpreted as a perfectly matched comparison. With the production minimum gene coverage of 25 counts, paired ILR testing gives 27.2\% rejection at concentration 32 and 9.5\% at concentration 1 on 2,048 count-null draws from 128 gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses spanning 121 blocks. Production-style cross-block variance moderation increases these to 29.0\% and 11.7\%, respectively. Baseline-centered paired testing gives 4.2\% at concentration 32 before moderation and 4.4\% afterward, retaining failed tests as \(p=1\), with 1,898 of 2,048 tests converged. These conditional screens are not direct estimates of Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}'s final gene-family false-discovery rate.

An experimental alternative fits one common null path mixture within each subject, evaluates unpenalized EC scores for each cell type, and projects away the common-mixture nuisance score before regularizing the contrast denominator. Let \(\mathbf u_{uc}\in\mathbb R^{S-1}\) and \(\mathbf I_{uc}\in\mathbb R^{(S-1)\times(S-1)}\) be the cell-type likelihood score and expected information at that subject's fitted null. For a pair, write \(\mathbf I_{u+}=\mathbf I_{u0}+\mathbf I_{u1}\). The efficient score and information are \begin{equation}\mathbf u_u^{\rm eff}=\mathbf u_{u1}-\mathbf I_{u1}\mathbf I_{u+}^{-1}(\mathbf u_{u0}+\mathbf u_{u1}),\qquad \mathbf I_u^{\rm eff}=\mathbf I_{u1}-\mathbf I_{u1}\mathbf I_{u+}^{-1}\mathbf I_{u1}.\end{equation} With null path proportions \(\boldsymbol\psi_u\in\mathbb R^S\), orthonormal basis \(\mathbf H\in\mathbb R^{S\times(S-1)}\), and denominator regularization \(\lambda\geq0\), the one-step response is \begin{equation}\widetilde{\mathbf d}_u=\left[\mathbf I_u^{\rm eff}+\lambda\mathbf H^\top\{\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol\psi_u)-\boldsymbol\psi_u\boldsymbol\psi_u^\top\}\mathbf H\right]^{-1}\mathbf u_u^{\rm eff}.\end{equation} The prior affects the common null fit and the denominator, but its gradient is not added to the cell-type contrast. On the production-cohort paired count nulls, these responses give 4.3\% rejection for \(\lambda=32\) and null-fit concentration 1, or 4.5\% with variance moderation; freeing the subject-null transcript shares gives 4.3\% and 4.4\%. All 2,048 score tests converge. These are near-nominal conditional-null results, not certification under arbitrary biological heterogeneity, annotation misspecification, or EB selection. The prototype's reported usage is a one-step softmax displacement around the subject-null mixture, not a separately quantified cell-type posterior mean. In matched real-data paired testing, baseline centering, subject centering, and both score variants yield zero replicated BH genes on the 457-gene SUPPA2 universe. Their all-tested LR A100 values under continuous raw ranking are 78.5\%, 74.9\%, 76.3\%, and 77.4\%, respectively. These new all-tested mappings have different eligible universes from the reporting-only audit, and are not improvements over the comparator on both endpoints. None is adopted as production.

The multilevel score prototype tests all cell types jointly rather than averaging separately tested pairs. Let \(\mathbf h_c\in\mathbb R^{C-1}\) be the transpose of row \(c\) of an orthonormal cell-type contrast basis, \(D=(C-1)(S-1)\), \(\mathbf v_u=\sum_c\mathbf h_c\otimes\mathbf u_{uc}\in\mathbb R^D\), \(\mathbf B_u=\sum_c\mathbf h_c\otimes\mathbf I_{uc}\in\mathbb R^{D\times(S-1)}\), and \(\mathbf I_{u+}=\sum_c\mathbf I_{uc}\); sums include the cell types observed for that subject. The nuisance-projected cluster score and information are \begin{equation}\mathbf g_u=\mathbf v_u-\mathbf B_u\mathbf I_{u+}^{+}\sum_c\mathbf u_{uc},\qquad \mathbf J_u=\sum_c(\mathbf h_c\mathbf h_c^\top)\otimes\mathbf I_{uc}-\mathbf B_u\mathbf I_{u+}^{+}\mathbf B_u^\top.\end{equation} Here \(^{+}\) denotes a Moore--Penrose inverse on identifiable directions. The denominator penalty \(\mathbf P_u\in\mathbb R^{D\times D}\) and omnibus statistic are \begin{equation}\begin{aligned}\mathbf P_u&=\mathbf I_{C-1}\otimes\mathbf H^\top\{\operatorname{diag}(\boldsymbol\psi_u)-\boldsymbol\psi_u\boldsymbol\psi_u^\top\}\mathbf H,\\T&=\left(\sum_u\mathbf g_u\right)^\top\left[\sum_u\mathbf J_u+\lambda\sum_u\mathbf P_u\right]^{+}\left(\sum_u\mathbf g_u\right).\end{aligned}\end{equation} A subject-cluster wild reference applies one independent Rademacher sign to each complete \(\mathbf g_u\), retaining dependence among its cell-type contrasts; the audit uses 4,095 draws and counts ties conservatively. This avoids treating depth-dependent, separately regularized one-step responses as exchangeable biological effects. It still conditions on within-path transcript shares and relies on approximately symmetric independent subject scores under the null. Count-null, subject-split, and all-tested LR assessments are required before this exploratory test can replace the current omnibus procedure. Testing strength is held at 32 in each subject half and 64 in full data for both the new and refitted uniform controls, not tuned using LR agreement.

On a common panel of 1,916 successfully assessed count-null draws spanning 120 blocks, the uniform concentration-32 omnibus control rejects 45.6\% at nominal .05, whereas the subject-score wild test rejects 4.9\% at \(\lambda=1\) and 4.6\% at \(\lambda=32\), after pooled calibration. The real-data split audit keeps the fixed control universe and assigns \(p=1\) to missing experimental fits. On 1,069 common blocks, the uniform control has 357 reproducible BH blocks, compared with zero and seven for the two score variants. Baseline-centered omnibus Wald testing gives 11 reproducible blocks on this same split universe. These are block-level audit counts, not Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}'s comparator-matched gene counts. Full-data remapping without a historical paired-discovery screen gives 615 LR-evaluable blocks for the score test; \(A_{100}\) is 66.1\% for \(\lambda=1\) and 64.5\% for \(\lambda=64\) under continuous raw-tail ranking, with the largest source concentration-1 effect choosing the cell-type contrast before LR assessment. The baseline-centered Wald audit gives 68.9\% on its own 650 LR-evaluable blocks, which is not an identical LR denominator. Both changes improve the conditional-null screen but fail to retain discovery reproducibility or establish an LR advantage. They remain exploratory, not a replacement for production.

\subsubsection*{Null-expectation correction of smoothed path contrasts}

A further experimental procedure retains strong smoothing but subtracts the contrast that the same estimator produces on expected counts under a target-path null. Unlike a common whole-transcript mixture, this null allows cell-type-specific transcript shares within each path and cell-type-specific outside-block abundance. For one subject with \(C\) observed types, \(T\) transcripts and \(S\geq2\) paths, let \(\boldsymbol\psi^0\in\Delta^{S-1}\) be the shared path composition, \(h_c\in(0,1]\) the block mass, and \(\boldsymbol\xi_{cs}\in\Delta^{m_s-1}\) the type-specific mixture of the \(m_s\) transcripts assigned to path \(s\). If \(m_{\mathrm{out}}\) outside transcripts exist, \(\boldsymbol\xi_{c\mathrm{out}}\in\Delta^{m_{\mathrm{out}}-1}\) is their conditional mixture. The null transcript vector \(\boldsymbol\theta_c^0\in\Delta^{T-1}\) has entries \begin{equation}\theta^0_{ct}=\begin{cases}h_c\psi^0_s\xi_{cs,t},&t\text{ belongs to path }s,\\(1-h_c)\xi_{c\mathrm{out},t},&t\text{ is outside the block}.\end{cases}\end{equation} Without outside transcripts, \(h_c=1\). Orthonormal log-ratio coordinates for each simplex and one block-mass logit when needed give \((S-1)+C(T-S)\) parameters. The primer-conditioned EC likelihood fits this null jointly across types. The shared target composition has no smoothing prior; total pseudocount \(10^{-4}\) within each nontrivial nuisance mixture stabilizes its fit without introducing a target-path prior. Analytic gradients account for every primer normalizer, and failed subject fits invalidate the hypothesis rather than silently removing difficult subjects.

For primer map \(\mathbf D^{(p)}\in\mathbb R_+^{K_p\times T}\), observed counts \(\mathbf y_{uc}^{(p)}\in\mathbb N_0^{K_p}\), and their total \(n_{uc}^{(p)}\), define expected null counts \(\widetilde{\mathbf y}_{uc}^{(p)}=n_{uc}^{(p)}\mathbf D^{(p)}\widehat{\boldsymbol\theta}_{uc}^0/\{\mathbf1^\top\mathbf D^{(p)}\widehat{\boldsymbol\theta}_{uc}^0\}\in\mathbb R_+^{K_p}\). These preserve each subject--type--primer total, without rounding or pooling primers. Let \(f_\zeta\) denote the free-transcript path estimator with total uniform path concentration \(\zeta\), and \(\mathbf H_S\in\mathbb R^{S\times(S-1)}\) an orthonormal sum-zero basis. Its corrected response is \begin{equation}\mathbf r_{uc}=\mathbf H_S^\top\left[f_\zeta(\{\mathbf y_{uc}^{(p)}\}_p)-f_\zeta(\{\widetilde{\mathbf y}_{uc}^{(p)}\}_p)\right]\in\mathbb R^{S-1},\qquad \mathbf d_u=\mathbf r_{u1}-\mathbf r_{u0}.\end{equation} This is a linear proportion contrast, not an ILR and not a simplex-constrained reported usage. In a direct categorical-count model, the correction removes the deterministic unequal-depth smoothing contrast exactly. For general EC maps, evaluating an estimator at expected counts need not equal its finite-count expectation, so this is not an exact bias correction or a substitute for independent count-null validation. Paired testing uses the existing \(t\)/Hotelling test, with scalar variance moderation and sign-flip calibration in the full benchmark. Reporting uses a separate free-transcript concentration-1 arithmetic subject mean; its failures do not justify shrinking the tested family.

On 32 independently selected production-eligible paired hypotheses with two count draws each, concentration-32 raw nominal-.05 rejection is 2/64 without biological type variation, 3/64 with biological precision 20, and 4/64 with type-specific within-path transcript tilts of standard deviation one. The corresponding converged counts are 61, 62 and 61; failed cases remain \(p=1\). With cross-block variance moderation, raw rejection is 2/64, 3/64 and 5/64, and pooled-calibrated rejection is 3/64, 3/64 and 5/64. These small panels improve over the preceding strong-prior count-null controls but do not certify extreme tails, event dependence or real-data FDR. Applying the same responses to subject-blocked omnibus regression gives concentration-32 trace-F raw rejection 2/64, 3/64 and 5/64, versus calibrated 2/64, 2/64 and 3/64, with 55, 56 and 55 converged trials. Pillai and maximum-coordinate diagnostics remain exploratory; their assumptions and failures are not removed by the response correction.

The complete paired benchmark fixes \(\zeta=32\) in both halves and full data, retaining 7,671, 5,140 and 11,462 candidates, including all failures. It yields 221, 156 and 611 event-level BH calls. On the frozen 457-gene SUPPA2 universe, gene-level conjunction BH still gives zero replicated genes after calibration. Selected event-union directions agree in 176/182 cases for the corrected testing response, but only 155/182 for the separately reported concentration-1 usage effect, 96.7\% versus 85.2\%; these are variant-selected events, not the older 1,171-event reporting union. Complete long-read remapping gives 2,059 evaluable associations, top-100 agreement 64\%, and \(A_{100}=63.7\%\) with calibrated-then-raw ordering or 67.6\% with continuous raw ordering. This is below SUPPA2's 90.7\% on its own ranked event universe. Null correction therefore does not meet the joint split/LR objective and is not adopted in the main results.

An independently checked native-tail diagnostic isolates the pooled calibration floor without changing fitted statistics. On the production cohort, conditional EC-score testing has 68/39 native event BH calls in the two halves versus 53/36 after calibration on the identical complete BH family; its SUPPA2-matched replicated-gene count increases only from zero to four. The corrected-proportion procedure has 233/155 native event calls versus 221/156 calibrated, and seven rather than zero replicated genes on that same SUPPA2 universe. Thus finite pooled-tail resolution can erase some gene-level conjunction calls, but removing calibration does not establish the desired power or external agreement. Native tails remain diagnostic, not an adopted fix. Every source shard is checked against its recorded production-cohort settings, and its full-data calibrated p-values must match the reused LR mapping before this comparison is allowed.

\subsubsection*{Uncertainty-weighted likelihood scores with transcript nuisance}

An experimental extension retains the preceding shared-path null but tests its unpenalized EC likelihood scores instead of smoothed path estimates. Let \(C\ge2\) be the number of cell types, \(S\ge2\) the number of paths, \(T\ge S\) the number of represented transcripts, and \(D=(C-1)(S-1)\) the dimension of reference-coded cell-type ILR contrasts \(\boldsymbol\beta\in\mathbb R^D\). Each subject has a common null path composition and independent within-path transcript mixtures and outside-block mass for every observed type. Only the nuisance mixtures receive the numerical total pseudocount \(10^{-4}\); the target path null and tested contrast receive no smoothing prior. The null parameter dimension is \((S-1)+C_u(T-S)\), where \(C_u\) is the number of represented types in subject \(u\).

For subject \(u\), collect the observed EC residuals divided by their null expected-count standard deviations in \(\mathbf e_u\in\mathbb R^{R_u}\), where \(R_u\) is the total number of positive-probability EC entries across its observed types and primers. Differentiating the primer-normalized EC probabilities and applying the same expected-count whitening gives target columns \(\mathbf Z_u\in\mathbb R^{R_u\times D}\) and null nuisance columns \(\mathbf N_u\in\mathbb R^{R_u\times[(S-1)+C_u(T-S)]}\). The latter include shared path usage, independent within-path mixtures and outside mass. An analytic linear-transcript-simplex basis avoids a numerical ILR-Jacobian rank failure when a path is nearly absent. With \(\mathbf Q_u\) an orthonormal basis for the column space of \(\mathbf N_u\), define \begin{equation}\mathbf Z_u^\perp=(\mathbf I_{R_u}-\mathbf Q_u\mathbf Q_u^\top)\mathbf Z_u,\qquad \mathbf g_u=(\mathbf Z_u^\perp)^\top\mathbf e_u\in\mathbb R^D,\qquad \mathbf J_u=(\mathbf Z_u^\perp)^\top\mathbf Z_u^\perp\in\mathbb R^{D\times D}.\end{equation} This orthogonal projection profiles likelihood nuisance rather than attributing all gene-wide EC information to the selected path contrast. Some contrasts remain genuinely unidentifiable after this projection; a prior is not counted as observation information.

The working random-effects score model is \begin{equation}\mathbf g_u\sim\mathcal N\!\left(\mathbf J_u\boldsymbol\beta,\ \mathbf J_u+\tau^2\mathbf J_u\mathbf B_u\mathbf J_u\right),\qquad \mathbf B_u=(\mathbf I_{C-1}+\mathbf1\mathbf1^\top)\otimes\left[\mathbf H_S^\top\operatorname{diag}\{1/\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_u^0\}\mathbf H_S\right].\end{equation} Here \(\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_u^0\in(0,1)^S\) is the shared subject null composition, \(\mathbf B_u\in\mathbb R^{D\times D}\) is a Dirichlet-inspired ILR contrast covariance shape, and \(\tau^2\ge0\) is a shared biological variance multiplier for the hypothesis. This is a Gaussian approximation, not an exact Dirichlet--multinomial or beta-binomial likelihood. The reference-sharing term \(\mathbf1\mathbf1^\top\) preserves covariance among cell-type contrasts. An information eigendecomposition retains only measured directions; missing directions never acquire zero measurement variance. Restricted maximum likelihood jointly weights these directions and estimates \(\tau^2\), explicitly comparing the boundary \(\tau^2=0\). Consequently a noisy subject response is not treated as equivalent to a precise one, and the gain of the count likelihood is retained rather than canceled by an arbitrary denominator prior.

Let \(\widehat{\boldsymbol\beta}\in\mathbb R^D\) and \(\mathbf V_\beta\in\mathbb R^{D\times D}\) denote the fitted mean and its conditional covariance, \(M\) the number of informative subject clusters, \(r_u=\operatorname{rank}(\mathbf J_u)\), and \(Q_{\rm res}\) the fitted weighted residual sum of squares. The prespecified small-sample diagnostic uses \begin{equation}q^\star=\max\left\{1,\frac{Q_{\rm res}}{\sum_u r_u-D}\right\},\qquad F=\frac{\widehat{\boldsymbol\beta}^\top\mathbf V_\beta^{-1}\widehat{\boldsymbol\beta}}{Dq^\star},\qquad p=\Pr\{F_{D,M-1}\ge F\}.\end{equation} In the scalar full-rank case this is the modified Knapp--Hartung adjustment discussed by \href{https://doi.org/10.1186/s12874-015-0091-1}{R\"over, Knapp and Friede, 2015}. Its extension to multivariate estimated EC scores is approximate, not an exact finite-count or finite-subject theorem. A chi-square reference and a residual-degrees-of-freedom F reference are retained as diagnostics. Whole-hypothesis fitting failures remain \(p=1\) on the complete declared family. Sign-flipping a whole subject score vector preserves its internal contrast dependence, but does not validate Gaussian count approximation, information estimation or asymmetric biological nulls.

Using the same 32 production-eligible hypotheses and two count draws per scenario, the cluster-F raw nominal-.05 rejection counts are 0/64, 2/64 and 0/64 for paired strict, biological-precision-20 and type-nuisance-shift nulls, with 54, 51 and 51 successful cases. The corresponding pooled-calibrated counts are 5/64, 2/64 and 2/64. Omnibus raw counts are 0/64, 2/64 and 0/64, with 46, 46 and 52 successful cases; calibrated counts are 2/64, 2/64 and 5/64. The fewer successful cases include insufficient identifiable subject contrasts, not solely optimizer failures. Conservatism and missing information can therefore contribute to apparent calibration. These small panels do not certify extreme tails or family FDR. Both real-data endpoints must be assessed before adoption.

Reporting is evaluated separately with two estimators, independently fitted concentration-1 arithmetic mean usage, and the fitted Gaussian contrast mapped back to the simplex. For the latter paired diagnostic, define a common source anchor \(\mathbf a=(\bar{\boldsymbol\psi}_a+\bar{\boldsymbol\psi}_b)/2\in[0,1]^S\) from the weak-prior subject means, and \(\boldsymbol\delta=\widehat{\boldsymbol\beta}\in\mathbb R^{S-1}\). Its two approximate usages are \(\operatorname{softmax}(\log\mathbf a\mp\mathbf H_S\boldsymbol\delta/2)\), after numerical clipping of anchor coordinates to \(10^{-12}\). This is a Gaussian one-step contrast representation, not a separately fitted EC posterior composition. The statistical p-values, complete tested families and rankings are identical for both reporting checks, with missing weak reports unavailable rather than averaged away; neither estimator is chosen using long-read outcomes.

The complete real-data run retains all 7,671, 5,140 and 11,462 paired candidates in the two halves and full data. The respective eligible counts are 6,568, 4,361 and 9,766, with all other candidates assigned \(p=1\); calibrated event-BH calls are 89, 42 and 196. On the frozen SUPPA2-matched 457-gene universe there are no fold-level gene-BH calls and no conjunction-BH replicated genes. The variant's own selected event union has 60 events, with 59 evaluable reported directions agreeing in all 59 cases under the independent weak-prior usage estimator, versus 57/59 under the fitted score contrast. This smaller selected family does not demonstrate preservation of the old discovery rate. Complete LR remapping has 1,917 evaluable associations. Weak-prior reporting gives calibrated-ranked \(A_{100}=75.3\%\) and raw-ranked \(A_{100}=77.1\%\), with top-100 agreement 64\% and 72\%. The fitted score contrast gives 69.2\% and 72.5\% for the corresponding areas, not an improvement. SUPPA2's own ranked LR area remains 90.7\%. Uncertainty weighting therefore improves neither endpoint enough to meet the joint target and is not adopted.

Using the identical successful fits and complete declared families, an uncalibrated cluster-F tail gives four SUPPA2-matched replicated genes, and the chi-square tail gives eleven, versus zero after pooled cluster-F calibration and 21 for the corrected SUPPA2 comparator. The chi-square tail yields 209/84 half-level event-BH calls, versus the calibrated cluster-F procedure's 89/42, but its LR \(A_{100}\) is still 77.5\%. Thus the small-sample reference and pooled calibration affect discovery power, but replacing them alone does not recover the comparator's two-endpoint performance. These analytic-tail checks remain diagnostics, not production inference; nominal count-null panels do not validate their extreme tails or family FDR.

\subsubsection*{Independent subject-split assessment of reporting}

The reporting alternatives are refitted independently in each subject half, including that half's pooled transcript baseline. Statistical tests and the existing matched event-BH selections remain frozen. We assess the union of events significant in either half, align paths by their genomic signatures, and compare the two path-proportion difference vectors by positive inner product. This reports descriptive effects at concentration 1, rather than the concentration-32 testing ILR effects used for the direction column in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}. It therefore does not replace or inherit that column's approximately 99\% agreement. The concentration-1 equal-subject refit is the appropriate control for concentration-1 pooling, and numerical baseline fitting is held common across these reporting alternatives.

On the 1,171 selected events in the SUPPA2-matched universe, equal-subject reporting agrees in 912 events (77.9\%), pooled reporting in 720 (61.5\%), and primer-balanced pooling in 696 (59.4\%). The respective Spearman correlations between aligned effect components are .615, .363, and .300. Pooling restricted to successfully quantified paired subjects gives the same values here, so inclusion of unpaired observations does not explain this deterioration. Across the seven comparator-matched universes, equal-subject agreement ranges from 77.9\% to 81.7\%, pooled agreement from 61.5\% to 66.1\%, and balanced pooled agreement from 59.4\% to 63.3\%. In the top 100 calls ranked independently in each discovery half, calibrated-first ordering gives 94\% and 91\% equal-subject direction agreement, compared with 50\% and 46\% pooled agreement and 45\% and 44\% balanced pooled agreement. Their held-half testing p-values are unchanged. Thus the pooled long-read improvement does not preserve the reproducibility of the reported directions, despite preserving discovery reproducibility mechanically, and pooled reporting is not adopted.

This decline is not caused by rounding of exactly zero effects: none of these selected SUPPA2-matched vectors has norm below \(10^{-12}\) in either half. A secondary assessment applies effect-magnitude thresholds in the discovery half only, without filtering the held half. Larger estimated effects agree more often, but the pooling gain on long-read data does not provide a uniform improvement in subject-split reporting. Different read weights in the two cell types can prevent cancellation of subject-specific baseline usage even when every subject is paired; simply pooling paired subjects does not restore an equal-subject contrast.

\subsubsection*{Uncertainty-aware Dirichlet reporting sensitivity}

A subject-level Dirichlet model can limit the leverage of deeply sequenced subjects by separating measurement noise from between-subject heterogeneity. However, estimated splice-path proportions are not observed multinomial counts. We assess a covariance-matched approximation, not an exact Dirichlet-multinomial replacement for the EC likelihood. For observation \(i\), let \(\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i\in\mathbb R^S\) be its concentration-1 path proportions, \(\mathbf V_i\in\mathbb R^{S\times S}\) their propagated conditional EC covariance, and \(n_i\) its EC count total. If \(\mathbf H\in\mathbb R^{S\times(S-1)}\) is the orthonormal ILR basis and \(\mathbf C_i\in\mathbb R^{(S-1)\times(S-1)}\) is the fitted ILR covariance, propagation uses \(\mathbf V_i=\mathbf J_i\mathbf C_i\mathbf J_i^\top\), with \(\mathbf J_i=(\operatorname{diag}(\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i)-\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i^\top)\mathbf H\). Matching the multinomial covariance trace defines an effective depth and fractional effective counts, \begin{equation}n_i^{\mathrm{eff}}=\min\left\{n_i,\frac{1-\|\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i\|_2^2}{\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf V_i)}\right\},\qquad \mathbf x_i=n_i^{\mathrm{eff}}\widehat{\boldsymbol\psi}_i.\end{equation} Unidentifiable or nonpositive-uncertainty observations are not silently treated as precise counts.

The population mean for cell type \(c\) is \(\boldsymbol\mu_c\in\mathbb R^S\), and one positive heterogeneity concentration \(\kappa_b\) is fitted for each block--cell-type contrast, shared between its two cell types. This is not the global statistical pseudocount concentration \(A\). The latent reporting model is \(\boldsymbol\psi_i\sim\operatorname{Dirichlet}(\kappa_b\boldsymbol\mu_{c(i)})\). Using gamma functions for fractional effective counts gives the approximate objective, up to terms independent of its parameters, \begin{equation}\begin{aligned}\ell(\kappa_b,\{\boldsymbol\mu_c\})&=\sum_i\{\log\Gamma(\kappa_b)-\log\Gamma(\kappa_b+n_i^{\mathrm{eff}})\}\\&\quad+\sum_{i,s}\{\log\Gamma(\kappa_b\mu_{c(i),s}+x_{is})-\log\Gamma(\kappa_b\mu_{c(i),s})\}\\&\quad+\frac1S\sum_{c,s}\log\mu_{cs}.\end{aligned}\end{equation} The last term supplies total uniform population-mean pseudocount 1. For two paths this is a beta-binomial-type approximation. Its variance-based subject precision weight is proportional to \(n_i^{\mathrm{eff}}(\kappa_b+1)/(n_i^{\mathrm{eff}}+\kappa_b)\), which approaches a finite limit as depth grows. The approximation discards anisotropic EC covariance, does not model paired biological dependence between a subject's cell types, and conditions on the pooled transcript baseline. It supplies reporting means, not biological p-values or exact posterior intervals.

An operational sensitivity falls back to the equal-subject concentration-1 effect when pooling fails, rather than improving agreement by excluding failures. On the same 1,171 SUPPA2-matched events, this gives 932 agreements (79.6\%), effect-component correlation .646, and a fallback in at least one half for 112 events. With continuous raw-tail ranking, its full-data long-read \(A_{100}\) is 73.6\%, compared with 73.0\% for equal-subject reporting and 80.0\% for primer-balanced pooling. Subject-aware pooling therefore largely retains equal-subject reproducibility but does not retain the pooled estimator's long-read gain. It remains exploratory. The heterogeneity concentration is fitted separately for each block--cell-type contrast; it is shared between the two cell types in that fit, not fixed globally across genes.

Further paired reporting sensitivities apply the identical weight to both members of each subject pair, so subject baseline usage cancels before aggregation. Harmonic-depth weights are proportional to \((n_{u0}^{-1}+n_{u1}^{-1})^{-1}\), where \(n_{uc}>0\) is the observed EC depth; they give 76.7\% split agreement and 73.0\% LR \(A_{100}\). An isotropic random-effects approximation matches the trace of the paired proportion covariance in the \(S-1\) dimensional simplex tangent space, estimates between-subject variance by restricted likelihood, and uses inverse total-variance weights; it gives 74.0\% split agreement and 70.6\% LR \(A_{100}\), with one explicit equal-subject fallback. Freeing all within-path and outside-block transcript weights gives 77.7\% split agreement and 74.1\% LR \(A_{100}\) with equal-subject concentration-1 reporting. None retains the pooled long-read gain. Increasing arithmetic reporting concentration to 4 raises split agreement to 93.1\% but decreases LR \(A_{100}\) to 72.5\%; the coverage-conditioned null mechanism above prevents treating restored split agreement alone as evidence of a biological improvement. All these comparisons use the production coverage filters and frozen statistical selections. The arithmetic concentration-1 control retains exactly the same subject counts and reproduces the earlier effects within maximum absolute component differences \(1.2\times10^{-5}\) and \(5.1\times10^{-7}\) in the two halves. This separates optimizer tolerance from changes to the eligible cohort.
