      \State Update \(B_g,\mu_g,\log s_g,\log\sigma_g\) against the bounded ELBO; damp the global update when the bound decreases.
    \Until{the ELBO gradient tolerance or iteration limit is reached}
  \EndIf
  \State Draw fixed antithetic standard-normal samples and reparameterize \(u_g=\mu_g+s_g\mathbin\odot\epsilon\).
  \State Optimize the exact-likelihood Monte Carlo ELBO \(\mathcal L_1\).
  \State Warm-start from the ELBO fit and optimize \(\mathcal L_{0.5}\); report its effective importance-sample size.
\EndFor
\State Compare each method's null-to-alternative gain on its own objective; do not compare raw objective values across approximation families.
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Block-specific paired EC-count GLMM cell-type test}
\label{alg:block-count-glmm}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require Block \(b\), paired gene EC counts \(\bm c_{gip}\), maps \(A_{gp}\), condition design \(Z_b\), cell-type design \(W_b\), mouse indices, and \(R\) parametric-bootstrap replicates
\Ensure Empirically calibrated block p-value
\State Fit the label-independent pooled isoform baseline, construct the path-ILR Jacobian \(J_b\), set \(H_b=J_b^\prime(J_bJ_b^\prime)^{-1}\), and obtain \(N_b\) from \(\ker(J_b)\).
\State Form the null fixed-effect tensor from unrestricted condition-by-isoform coefficients \(Z_b\otimes I_{d_g}\) and cell-type nuisance columns \(W_{bij}(N_b)_{:s}\).
\State Fit the null full-covariance posterior and variance parameters.
\State Append the \((C_b^{\rm cell}-1)d_b\) columns \(W_{bij}(H_b)_{:s}\), warm-start all null parameters, and fit the observed alternative.
\State Set \(T_b\gets2(\mathcal L_{1b}-\mathcal L_{0b})\), using the same variational objective for both nested fits.
\For{\(r=1,\ldots,R\)}
  \State Draw mouse effects and optional pseudobulk effects from the fitted null; compute primer-specific EC probabilities.
  \State Generate multinomial or Dirichlet--multinomial EC counts while preserving every observed primer-specific pseudobulk total.
  \State Refit the null, warm-starting from the observed null, then append the block-effect columns and fit the nested alternative.
\EndFor
\State Pool converged bootstrap statistics by likelihood, tested dimension, and depth stratum, excluding block \(b\)'s replicates.
\State Compute the finite-pool rank p-value and assess the same rank calculation on every null replicate before applying BH.
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Matched semi-synthetic power benchmark}
\label{alg:cluster-power}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require Two-condition partitions, effect sizes \(\gamma\), and replicate count \(R_{\rm power}\)
\Ensure Asymptotic and null-calibrated power for each method
\ForAll{eligible blocks \(b\)}
  \State Draw and fix a unit ILR direction \(\bm v_b\).
  \For{\(r=1,\ldots,R_{\rm power}\)}
    \State Permute condition labels among mice.
    \ForAll{nonzero effect sizes \(\gamma\)}
      \State Set \(\bm y_{bmc}^{(r,\gamma)} =\bm y_{bmc}+\gamma z_{bmc}^{(r)}\bm v_b\).
      \State Map shifted ILRs to compositions and effective path counts.
      \State Fit GEE, GLMM, clustered GLS, and Dirichlet--multinomial tests.
    \EndFor
  \EndFor
\EndFor
\State Rank each alternative statistic against null statistics for its block and method, using the finite-permutation correction.
\State Report asymptotic and event-matched permutation rejection at 0.05.
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Pairwise and dominant-path sensitivity tests}
\label{alg:sensitivities}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require Path estimates and conditional covariances from Algorithm~\ref{alg:local-fit}
\Ensure Calibrated sensitivity tables
\ForAll{cell-type pairs}
  \State Retain \(M\) mice containing both types.
  \State Use \(X_0\in\mathbb R^{2M\times M}\) for mouse effects and \(X_1\in\mathbb R^{2M\times(M+1)}\) for mouse plus cell type.
  \State Permute the two labels within mouse and correct across all block-by-pair hypotheses.
\EndFor
\ForAll{blocks \(b\)}
  \State Rank paths by \(\sum_iN_{bi}^{\rm gene}\psi_{bis}\) and select \(\mathcal S_b\), the two largest, without labels.
  \State Construct selector \(E_b\), two-path basis \(Q_b^{(2)}\), and \(\mathcal R_b=(Q_b^{(2)})^\prime E_b^\prime Q_b\).
  \State Set \(V_{bi}^{(2)}=\mathcal R_bV_{bi}\mathcal R_b^\prime\) and renormalize the two retained proportions.
  \State Run Algorithm~\ref{alg:compositional} and record path-floor sensitivity sweeps.
\EndFor
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\begin{algorithm}[H]
\caption{Joint condition tests}
\label{alg:joint}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require Calibrated block-by-cell-type p-values or ILR responses
\Ensure Partition- or gene-level joint p-values
\State For ACAT, combine valid cell-type p-values within a canonical partition; optionally combine partition p-values within a gene.
\State As a negative control, fit one shared condition effect while treating cell-type pseudobulks as independent.
\State For clustered GLS, construct diagonal covariance blocks \(V_{bmc}+(\tau_{\rm mouse}^2+\tau_{\rm resid}^2)I_{d_b}\) and same-mouse off-diagonal blocks \(\tau_{\rm mouse}^2I_{d_b}\).
\State Estimate \(\tau_{\rm mouse}^2\) and \(\tau_{\rm resid}^2\) by REML under the null.
\State Fit the shared condition coefficient by GLS.
\ForAll{mouse-level condition permutations}
  \State Apply one permuted condition to all cell types from that mouse and refit the model.
\EndFor
\State Apply BH to the canonical-partition family; keep negative-control and calibrated results separate.
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

\section{Supporting Implementation Details}

\subsection*{Splice-block construction}

GTF exons are represented by zero-based, half-open intervals. Exon boundaries define atomic intervals; intervals exonic in every annotated spliced isoform are merged into constitutive anchors. Consecutive anchors define a block, and an isoform path is its ordered exon-interval chain between them. Terminal pseudo-anchors include alternative first and last regions. A gene without a shared constitutive region receives one whole-gene block.

Blocks with one path are discarded. Simple two-path blocks include skipped exons, mutually exclusive exons, alternative donors and acceptors, and retained introns. The implementation also supports nested and multi-path blocks. Precursor isoforms remain nuisance isoforms in the EC model but are not assigned to spliced paths.

Different genomic blocks can induce exactly the same partition of annotated isoforms, for example when several exons always occur together. Such blocks have identical statistical responses and must not count as repeated evidence. A canonical partition is the unordered collection of isoform sets assigned to paths. Equivalent blocks are collapsed before FDR or joint testing; all member interval identifiers remain in the output.

\subsection*{Pseudobulks and local fitting}

Cell isoform proportions from the hierarchical decoder are weighted by cell UMI totals and aggregated by mouse, condition, and cell type. Pseudobulks must contain at least 20 cells and \(10^5\) total UMIs. A block is fit when its gene has at least 25 gene-assigned UMIs in at least two qualifying pseudobulks. Only ECs whose compatible isoforms map uniquely to that gene enter the local likelihood.

Bounded L-BFGS optimizes the paired multinomial likelihood, with \(\|\bm\delta_{bi}\|_\infty\leq20\) to prevent floating-point underflow, where \(\|\cdot\|_\infty\) is the maximum absolute component. Two-path fits are initialized from a log-ratio grid because the paired-primer normalizers can make the objective non-concave.

For the cell-resolved variant, sparse cell-by-EC matrices are retained and one local isoform matrix is decoded at a time. The optimizer dimension is still only the number of paths minus one. Cell-specific Fisher contributions are accumulated analytically, so the method does not fit one free splicing parameter per cell.

\subsection*{Identifiability and reliability}

Finite Fisher covariance is not sufficient near a simplex boundary. Parametric bootstrap identified two reliability requirements: \[
  \lambda_{\max}(V_{bi})\leq 1/8
  \quad\text{and}\quad
  \min_s\widehat\psi_{bis}\geq 0.15.
\]
Thus every tested log-ratio direction has effective information of at least eight and every fitted path has at least 15\% usage. All estimates are written, together with convergence, identifiability, and reliability flags; only reliable estimates enter GLS. Here \(\lambda_{\max}(V_{bi})\in\mathbb R_+\) is the largest eigenvalue of the \(d_b\times d_b\) covariance, and \(\widehat\psi_{bis}\in[0,1]\) is path \(s\)'s fitted proportion.

\section{Supporting Results and Benchmarks}

\subsection*{Condition-test scope}

The primary analysis used the 25-gene-UMI cutoff and blocks with at most eight represented paths. It fit 10,100 blocks and 359,713 pseudobulk-by-block path estimates; 359,711 fits converged. After reliability filtering, there were 829 conditional and 506 profile tests.

These 1,335 fits represent 829 distinct block-by-cell-type hypotheses, covering 527 distinct splice blocks in eight cell types. The profile analysis is available for a subset of the conditional hypotheses. The possible conditions were 5xFAD, 5xFIRE, 5xFIRE PBS, 5xFIRE transplant, FIRE, and WT. For the 829 conditional hypotheses, 275 compared two conditions, 155 compared three, 153 compared four, 130 compared five, and 116 compared all six. The included set varies because every condition in a test must have at least three independent mouse pseudobulks for that block and cell type.

\subsection*{Condition-test results}

No block was significant at 5\% FDR in either covariance analysis. At the unadjusted \(p<0.05\) level:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=*]
  \item the conditional analysis identified 24 block-by-cell-type hypotheses, covering 21 distinct blocks;
  \item the profile analysis identified 20 block-by-cell-type hypotheses, covering 17 distinct blocks;
  \item 20 hypotheses were nominally significant in both analyses.
\end{itemize}
The union is 24 nominal block-by-cell-type hypotheses covering 21 blocks, but the corrected number of significant hypotheses and blocks is zero. Thus there is no FDR-significant evidence for condition-dependent splicing within cell type. The two covariance variants should not be added as if they were separate discoveries.

\subsection*{Formal mouse-clustered models}

The shared-condition analysis was repeated with the multinomial-logit GLMM and Liang--Zeger GEE defined above. Clustered Gaussian ILR GLS and the unclustered Dirichlet--multinomial were refit to the same canonical partitions as comparators. Each block used 50 synchronized mouse-level null permutations. For 88 two-condition blocks, 50 semi-synthetic replicates were generated at each ILR effect size; 4,369 of 4,400 replicate designs had full rank. ``Perm. nominal'' below is the number of observed partitions with an event-matched permutation p-value at most 0.05. Table~\ref{tab:clustered-power} summarizes the complete analysis.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Calibration and power comparison. Null rejection uses asymptotic p-values on permuted labels; power uses event-matched permutation ranks.}
\label{tab:clustered-power}
\begin{tabular}{lrrrrrr}
\toprule
Method & Tests & Conv. & Null $<.05$ & Perm. nominal & Power $.25$ & Power $.50$\\
\midrule
Clustered GLS & 359 & 359 & 0.025 & 17 & 0.171 & 0.322\\
Dirichlet--multinomial & 359 & 359 & 0.138 & 18 & 0.188 & 0.364\\
Liang--Zeger GEE & 359 & 355 & 0.415 & 8 & 0.139 & 0.268\\
Laplace GLMM & 359 & 359 & 0.108 & 19 & 0.199 & 0.390\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

The canonical GEE sandwich reference is unusable at the available mouse counts: 41.5\% of permuted-null p-values were below 0.05, and its apparent 116 asymptotic FDR discoveries are artifacts of this inflation. The GLMM reference was closer but still anti-conservative at 10.8\%, while clustered GLS was conservative at 2.5\%. Event-matched permutation calibration is therefore required for all comparisons. On that scale, the GLMM had the highest power, improving over clustered GLS by 2.8 and 6.9 percentage points at ILR effects 0.25 and 0.5. GEE was less powerful than clustered GLS after calibration. No method produced a permutation-calibrated 5\% FDR discovery in the observed condition analysis.

\subsection*{EC-count GLMM inference benchmark}

The EC-count methods were first compared in 96 independent simulations per likelihood and effect size. Each replicate had 12 mice, two observations per mouse, two isoforms, two distinct three-EC primer maps, 150 molecules per primer and observation, and mouse random-effect standard deviation 0.4. Dirichlet--multinomial simulations used concentration 30. The null threshold for each method was its own 95th percentile evidence gain at effect zero; power was evaluated at an isoform logit effect of 0.5. This calibration is required because Laplace likelihoods, tilted ELBOs, and R\'enyi bounds are not numerically interchangeable objectives.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\caption{Ambiguous-EC simulation comparison. RMSE is for the condition logit coefficient. Time is median seconds per nested null/alternative fit at effect 0.5. ESS is the median alternative mouse-level importance ESS out of 128.}
\label{tab:ec-glmm-simulation}
\begin{tabular}{llrrrrr}
\toprule
Likelihood & Approximation & Null rejection & Power & RMSE & Time & ESS\\
\midrule
Multinomial & Laplace & 0.052 & 0.542 & 0.223 & 9.87 & --\\
Multinomial & Tilted ELBO & 0.052 & 0.542 & 0.223 & 1.87 & --\\
Multinomial & R\'enyi $\alpha=0.5$ & 0.052 & 0.542 & 0.223 & 1.58 & 127.67\\
Dirichlet--multinomial & Laplace & 0.052 & 0.417 & 0.236 & 21.41 & --\\
Dirichlet--multinomial & Monte Carlo ELBO & 0.052 & 0.417 & 0.236 & 3.06 & 127.51\\
Dirichlet--multinomial & R\'enyi $\alpha=0.5$ & 0.052 & 0.417 & 0.236 & 3.35 & 127.66\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Within each likelihood family, all three approximations had the same empirically calibrated power to the reported precision, and their coefficient bias differed by less than \(2\times10^{-4}\). The tilted bound's null cutoff was 4.70, compared with 4.60 for multinomial Laplace and R\'enyi VI, but this small scale difference did not change rejection. Variational fitting was five- to seven-fold faster than Laplace. A single global importance weight over all mice had ESS 1/128 and collapsed the fitted random-effect variance; the mouse-factorized R\'enyi objective corrected this failure and produced ESS above 127.5/128 for both likelihoods.

A second simulation used three isoforms and otherwise retained the same 96 null and 96 effect replicates. Full-covariance and diagonal VI were nearly indistinguishable. Tilted evidence gains had Spearman correlation 0.9999 and median absolute difference 0.073 between posterior families; R\'enyi gains had correlation 0.99996 and median absolute difference 0.044. Every VI variant had calibrated power 0.438 and condition-coefficient RMSE 0.237--0.238. The full-covariance tilted fit took a median 2.43 seconds per nested effect fit versus 2.00 seconds for diagonal tilted VI. Thus, in a controlled multi-isoform setting, omitted posterior covariance was not a material source of error.

The same conclusion held for the direct EC Dirichlet--multinomial. In 96 three-isoform null and effect simulations, diagonal and full ELBO gains had correlation 0.9997 and median absolute difference 0.150; diagonal and full R\'enyi gains had correlation 0.9999 and median difference 0.092. Both posterior families had calibrated power 0.219. In the 65-gene real-data analysis, diagonal and full ELBO gains had correlations 0.998 for condition and 0.9998 for cell type, with median absolute differences 0.0035 and 0.0050. Fixed-draw R\'enyi optimization was again less stable than ELBO optimization.

The genome-wide paired-primer comparison retained 65 genes with mean depth at least 100 EC UMIs per pseudobulk; this was 65 of 66 depth-eligible genes, with one 3,905-EC outlier excluded by the 128-EC computational cap. All retained genes had at most six EC-supported isoforms. The authoritative run initialized each Gaussian variational posterior from the corresponding Laplace means and covariance diagonal, then initialized R\'enyi VI from the ELBO fit. Across the 65 condition models, convergence was 98.5\% for both Laplace likelihoods, 95.4\% for both ELBO variants, 92.3\% for multinomial R\'enyi VI, and 98.5\% for Dirichlet--multinomial R\'enyi VI. Cell-type convergence ranged from 93.8\% to 100\%. There were no fit exceptions.

Approximation agreement depended on isoform dimension. Among the 38 two-isoform genes, multinomial Laplace and R\'enyi evidence gains had Spearman correlation 0.974 for condition and 0.9996 for cell type; Laplace and tilted ELBO correlations were 0.969 and 0.9995. All 16 two-isoform condition genes above the method-specific nominal threshold under multinomial Laplace were also above it under R\'enyi VI. For the 20 three-isoform genes, the analogous gain correlations fell to 0.49 and 0.59. The mean-field posterior has only a diagonal covariance across isoform random effects, whereas Laplace retains their within-mouse posterior covariance. The dimension-dependent divergence therefore motivated a full-covariance extension, but by itself did not distinguish covariance error from objective or optimizer differences. One-dimensional simulation thresholds were not applied to multi-isoform or cell-type omnibus tests.

The tilted surrogate must also be separated from objective evaluation. For two-isoform condition models, median nested gains were 5.54 for the tilted training bound, 4.95 for an exact-likelihood Monte Carlo ELBO evaluated at the same posterior, 4.93 for its R\'enyi evaluation, 4.93 for Laplace, and 4.95 for separately optimized R\'enyi VI. For cell type, the corresponding medians were 89.06, 74.38, 74.36, 72.12, and 74.36. Exact ELBO and R\'enyi gains on the same posterior had Spearman correlations 0.998 and 1.000 for condition and cell type. The tilted bound becomes looser under the larger alternative and therefore overstates nested evidence gains; it is retained as a fitting surrogate, not used as a likelihood-ratio statistic.

Full-covariance results did not support posterior covariance as the cause of the earlier multi-isoform discrepancy. On the real condition analysis, fresh-sample ELBO gains from diagonal and full tilted posteriors had Spearman correlation 0.991 across 65 genes and median absolute difference 0.002. Among the 20 three-isoform genes, the correlation was 0.938 and median difference was 0.024. The corresponding cell-type values were 1.000 overall and within the three-isoform subset, with median three-isoform difference 0.023. These results, together with the three-isoform simulation, point instead to differences in the Laplace objective or its outer optimization. Separately optimizing a fixed-draw full-covariance R\'enyi objective was less stable than fitting the tilted objective and evaluating both exact objectives with fresh draws; the latter is the reported comparison.

The observation-level logistic-normal model was validated with 96 null and 96 effect simulations using three isoforms, true residual standard deviation 0.6, and the same ambiguous paired-primer maps. Tilted full-covariance VI estimated median residual standard deviation 0.559 under the effect and had calibrated power 0.354. Dirichlet--multinomial Laplace had power 0.365, while the misspecified ordinary multinomial had power 0.313. Logistic-normal Laplace had power 0.146 despite estimating residual scale 0.472, indicating that its high-dimensional marginal approximation was not competitive with the tilted fit.

In the real data, tilted logistic-normal fits converged under both nested models for 80.0\% of condition genes and 86.2\% of cell-type genes. The alternative residual standard deviation had median 0.040 for condition and 0.029 for cell type; the respective interquartile ranges were 0.007--0.239 and 0.006--0.234. Relative to the multinomial full posterior, fresh ELBO condition gains changed by a median absolute 0.335 and retained rank correlation 0.809. Cell-type gains changed by 10.68 and retained correlation 0.959, consistent with extra-multinomial variation having more impact on the larger cell-type omnibus statistic. Because residual-scale estimates often approached zero and convergence was weaker, this model is retained as an overdispersion sensitivity analysis rather than replacing the primary test.

The initial block-specific EC analysis inherited 406 coverage-eligible partitions from the path-count analysis and tested 399 after EC identifiability filtering. This was unnecessarily restrictive because it required reliable path estimates before fitting the EC likelihood. The genome-wide EC screen instead includes one EC count vector for every pseudobulk--primer combination in the likelihood, retains cell types represented in at least three mice, and requires at least two represented cell types. The strict rule additionally requires at least 25 gene UMIs and 30 covered pseudobulks; the permissive rule requires at least 10 gene UMIs. These rules retain 1,926 and 5,650 blocks, respectively. Five parametric-bootstrap replicates per block provide pooled null distributions matched by tested dimension. Table~\ref{tab:initial-block-results} reports the calibrated tests.

\label{tab:initial-block-results}
\begin{tabular}{llrrrrrr}
\toprule
Coverage & Likelihood & Tests & Conv. & Nominal & FDR & Null $<.05$ & Depth range\\

The permissive rule increases absolute discoveries from 174 to 294 for MN and from 157 to 193 for LN, while testing 2.9 times as many blocks. MN and LN share 152 strict and 192 permissive discoveries. DM has no genome-wide FDR discoveries under either rule despite acceptable calibration. On the 1,926 shared hypotheses, applying BH only to that common family gives 316 MN, 265 LN, and 40 DM discoveries under the permissive fits, versus 174, 157, and zero under the strict fits. Thus lower-count pseudobulks add signal, but much of that gain is spent on the larger genome-wide hypothesis family. The corresponding strict-versus-permissive statistic rank correlations are 0.781, 0.764, and 0.760, so changing the cutoff also changes individual fits rather than only the BH denominator.

\subsection*{Expanded EC likelihood and coverage comparison}

The annotation-level block equivalence relation is insufficient after EC screening: removing unsupported isoforms can make two distinct annotated blocks induce exactly the same partition of the remaining isoforms. The final hypothesis family therefore retains one representative for each tuple of gene, EC-supported isoforms, canonical block-path partition, covered observations, and tested factor levels. This reduces the permissive cell-type family from 5,650 to 4,825 tests and the condition family from 17,685 to 14,833 block-by-cell-type tests over 5,608 representative blocks. This collapse is performed before calibration and BH correction.

The initial empirical calibration compared each test only with its degrees-of-freedom-specific bootstrap pool. After supported-partition deduplication, its minimum attainable p-values were approximately $4.5\times10^{-4}$, whereas BH requires resolution near $10^{-5}$ at the first ranks. Its zero logistic-normal calls were therefore a bootstrap-tail resolution artifact, not disappearance of the cell-type signal.

The former resolved-tail procedure replaced this initial calibration. Its nonredundant deprecated results are reported in Table~\ref{tab:deprecated-primary-ec-results}; the earlier annotation-level counts in Table~\ref{tab:initial-block-results} are retained only to document why supported-partition deduplication and resolved-tail calibration were required.

\subsection*{Cell-type effects within mouse}

The initial Gaussian analysis tested 369 conditional and 218 profile partitions. It found 22 and five FDR-significant partitions, respectively, with all profile discoveries contained in the conditional set. After canonicalizing annotation intervals that encode the same isoform-to-path partition, these 22 intervals represented 14 distinct partitions.

The compositional analysis used converged, conditionally identifiable estimates and required every modeled path proportion to be at least 5\%. This produced 646 nonredundant tests. A minimum of 30 pseudobulk observations per partition, chosen as a label-effect-independent coverage requirement, retained 406 hypotheses for FDR correction. Among these, 149 had exact permutation \(p<0.05\), and \textbf{101 splice-path partitions in 95 genes passed 5\% FDR}. Thirteen of the 14 nonredundant Gaussian discoveries were recovered; the remaining partition had exact \(p=0.032\) but FDR 0.098. Significant compositional results had median maximum cell-type logit effect 0.84 and median effective path count 21.7.

The observation-count threshold is not a narrow boundary effect: thresholds from 29 through 32 pseudobulks each gave 101 discoveries, while 33 gave 99. All 646 observed fits converged. The median number of valid permutations was 500; the minimum was 476 after excluding failed permutation refits. Asymptotic Dirichlet--multinomial p-values were anti-conservative, with 15.5\% of permutation-null values below 0.05, which is why only exact per-event permutation ranks are used for the primary result.

Two sensitivity analyses did not improve the event-level result. Testing 3,708 individual cell-type pairs gave 97 FDR-significant pair hypotheses but only 32 distinct partitions after correction across all pairs. Restricting each block to its two molecule-weighted dominant paths increased coverage; with a 1\% path floor it gave 88 discoveries, and removing the floor gave 91, under a cross-fitted empirical null. These analyses support the full-path exact omnibus test as the primary cell-type analysis.

Allowing the alternative to re-estimate \(\kappa\) also left the result unchanged: it found the same 101 partitions in the same 95 genes, with no event entering or leaving the 5\% FDR set. Exact p-values from fixed- and free-concentration fits had Spearman correlation 0.9995, and both had 148 nominal events. The null optimum was multinomial for 499 of 646 tests, and the alternative optimum was multinomial for 580. Among 66 tests with finite concentration in both models, the median alternative/null concentration ratio was 1.54, consistent with cell-type mean effects explaining some apparent overdispersion. Exact permutation calibration absorbs this nuisance-parameter flexibility, so freeing \(\kappa\) provides no net power gain here.

\subsection*{Calibration}

Across the 25-UMI analysis, 2.7\% of permuted-label Wald p-values were below 0.05 for both conditional and profile covariance, indicating conservative null calibration. A parametric bootstrap of 3,000 reliable local fits gave mean squared standardized error 1.10 and 94.5\% coverage for nominal 95\% intervals. Independent Gaussian simulations of REML--GLS gave type-I error between 2.7\% and 5.0\% across the tested biological-variance settings.

Numerical tests additionally verify skipped-exon block construction, identifiable covariance after collapsing indistinguishable isoforms, rejection of information-null path contrasts, the analytic binomial Fisher variance, and recovery of imposed GLS effects.

\subsection*{Coverage-cutoff sensitivity} \[
\begin{array}{rrrrrr}
\toprule
\text{UMIs} & \text{blocks} & \text{tests} &
\text{conditional null }p<.05 & \text{profile null }p<.05 &
\text{bootstrap coverage}\\
\midrule
25  & 10100 & 1335 & 0.027 & 0.027 & 0.945\\
50  &  5896 &  755 & 0.031 & 0.030 & 0.936\\
100 &  2820 &  283 & 0.029 & 0.025 & 0.946\\
200 &  1034 &   69 & 0.028 & 0.026 & 0.948\\
500 &   195 &   10 & 0.030 & 0.050 & 0.946\\
\bottomrule

\]
No cutoff produced an FDR-significant block. The 500-UMI calibration estimate is based on only 100 permutation tests.

For reliable two-condition, two-path conditional tests, mean asymptotic power for a 0.5 ILR effect was 0.49, 0.51, 0.44, 0.56, and 0.59 at increasing cutoffs. The final two values are selected from only eight and two eligible tests. Among 103 hypotheses shared by the 25- and 50-UMI analyses, power was 0.453 and 0.449. Lower coverage cutoffs therefore increase the number of testable blocks without degrading calibrated precision among shared tests. The 25-UMI cutoff is the default.

\subsection*{Does cell resolution increase condition-test power?}

The cell-resolved likelihood was run on the 527 blocks testable in the 25-UMI primary analysis. All 41,242 block-by-pseudobulk fits converged; 21,624 passed the reliability rules, compared with 21,010 pseudobulk conditional estimates on the same blocks. This produced 836 condition tests, compared with 829 for pseudobulk fitting. No cell-resolved test passed 5\% FDR. Condition-label permutations were conservative, with 2.49\% of null p-values below 0.05.

An exact matched comparison used the same reliable samples for both methods in 807 block-by-cell-type tests. Cell resolution reduced the median local covariance trace by 1.6\%, and the median path-response RMS difference was 0.033 ILR units. P-values were strongly concordant (Spearman rank correlation 0.958); 24 pseudobulk and 25 cell-resolved tests were nominally significant.

For the 259 matched two-condition, two-path tests, mean asymptotic power for a 0.5 ILR effect was 0.504 for both methods. The median ratio of cell-resolved to pseudobulk condition-coefficient standard error was 0.995. Retaining cell likelihood terms therefore did not materially increase power in this dataset. The aggregated EC counts and decoder baseline preserve nearly all relevant local information within a cell type, while residual between-mouse variation dominates the small reduction in inferential variance. Pseudobulk fitting remains the default; the cell-resolved method is available for datasets with stronger within-pseudobulk isoform heterogeneity.

\subsection*{Compositional and joint-test assessment}

Collapsing equivalent isoform partitions reduced the 829 conditional block-by-cell-type fits to 505 nonredundant tests over 317 partitions. The median effective path count was 32 molecules, or 62\% of gene UMIs; only 0.6\% of estimates reached the gene-UMI cap.

On the same 505 tests, conditional GLS had 12 nominal results and the Dirichlet--multinomial had 18. Neither had a 5\% FDR discovery. The Dirichlet--multinomial was conservatively calibrated: 3.76\% of 10,100 permuted-label p-values were below 0.05. In contrast, pure multinomial regression gave 197 nominal and 155 FDR results, but 39.1\% of its permutation p-values were below 0.05. UMI sampling alone therefore cannot represent the large between-mouse compositional variation. Output tables mark pure multinomial and naive shared-effect fits as invalid for inference while retaining them as calibration diagnostics.

ACAT across cell types produced 8 nominal GLS and 12 nominal Dirichlet--multinomial partitions among 317 tests, with no FDR discoveries. Combining distinct partitions within genes gave 7 and 11 nominal genes among 276 tests, again with no FDR discoveries.

Naive shared-effect pooling illustrates the pseudoreplication risk. It gave 43 nominal and one FDR Dirichlet--multinomial partition, but synchronized mouse permutations rejected 13.2\% of nulls at 0.05. The apparent discovery had asymptotic \(p=6.4\times10^{-5}\) but permutation \(p=0.143\). Naive shared GLS was also inflated, with 8.4\% null rejection.

The mouse-clustered shared GLS restored conservative calibration: 2.19\% of 7,169 synchronized permutation p-values were below 0.05. It produced 11 nominal partitions among 359 tests and no FDR discoveries; its smallest p-value was 0.0126 with FDR 0.86. Gene-level ACAT gave 9 nominal genes among 313 and no FDR discoveries. Thus the calibrated gain is limited to the increase from 12 to 18 nominal within-cell-type tests under the Dirichlet--multinomial. None of the evaluated strategies creates a genome-wide condition discovery in this dataset.

\subsection*{SUPPA2 event-statistic sensitivity and Tealeaf/SUPPA2 hybrid model}

The SUPPA2 statistic audit fixes the five-event-type catalogue, the original subject-fold manifests, transcript-TPM PSI, and the 2,342 gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses from 457 genes in Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility}. Each fold has 5,297 event tests on this matched universe. The original PSI effects and subject counts are checked against the archived results. The median finite and nonzero pair counts are both 24. For 24 untied, unanimously signed differences, the uncorrected-continuity normal approximation gives a minimum p-value of about \(1.8\times10^{-5}\), whereas the exact two-sided tail is \(2^{1-24}=1.19\times10^{-7}\). The approximation is therefore too conservative in precisely the extreme tail needed after gene-level aggregation. Two additional implementation defects were corrected, omission of the tied-rank variance correction and integer overflow in exact-tail counting for sufficiently many subjects. Neither defect is repaired by merely averaging tied ranks.

For event \(e\), let \(n_e\in\mathbb N_0\) be the number of finite nonzero subject pairs, let \(\bm x_e\in\mathbb R^{n_e}\) contain their paired PSI differences, and let \(\bm r_e\in\mathbb R^{n_e}\) contain their averaged absolute ranks. The positive-rank sum is \(W_e^+=\sum_{u=1}^{n_e}r_{eu}\mathbf1(x_{eu}>0)\), its conditional null mean is \(\tfrac12\sum_u r_{eu}\), and its variance is \(\tfrac14\sum_u r_{eu}^2\). The variance expression includes arbitrary ties. Under the symmetric paired null, the signs are independent fair Bernoulli variables \(B_u\in\{0,1\}\). Doubling ranks gives integer weights \(w_{eu}=2r_{eu}\); the distribution of \(\sum_u w_{eu}B_u\) is computed by probability recursion, \[q_u(s)=\tfrac12q_{u-1}(s)+\tfrac12q_{u-1}(s-w_{eu}),\qquad q_0(s)=\mathbf1(s=0).\] Here \(q_u(s)\in[0,1]\) is the probability mass after the first \(u\) weights, with zero mass outside its support. Recursing on probabilities avoids integer count overflow. The exact two-sided p-value is twice the lower-tail probability at \(\min(2W_e^+,\sum_u w_{eu}-2W_e^+)\), capped at one. Zero-only valid events receive p-value one; events with no finite pairs are untested.

Table~\ref{tab:suppa-statistic-sensitivity} uses the same within-pair Simes aggregation, across-pair gene Simes aggregation, two-fold conjunction, and gene BH correction for every statistic. Logit testing clips PSI to \([10^{-3},1-10^{-3}]\); arcsine testing uses \(\arcsin\sqrt\psi\). Moderated tests estimate the variance prior from all events meeting the eight-pair criterion within a contrast, before restricting to the matched benchmark. The corrected exact signed-rank test is the primary comparator because it repairs the chosen paired test rather than selecting a new statistic on discovery count. The long-read analyses retain their separately archived full-data rankings and therefore do not inherit the split-test changes.

\begin{table}[H]
\centering
\small
\caption{SUPPA2 event-statistic sensitivity on the fixed Table~\ref{tab:ec-glmm-reproducibility} universe. Replication counts are genes, not event tests.}
\label{tab:suppa-statistic-sensitivity}
\begin{tabular}{lrrr}
\toprule
Statistic & Fold 0 BH & Fold 1 BH & Replicated BH\\
\midrule
Corrected normal signed-rank & 26 & 22 & 1\\
Exact conditional signed-rank & 48 & 44 & 21\\
Paired $t$ on PSI & 34 & 38 & 18\\
Moderated paired $t$ on PSI & 29 & 28 & 13\\
Paired $t$ on logit PSI & 23 & 30 & 14\\
Moderated paired $t$ on logit PSI & 18 & 26 & 10\\
Paired $t$ on arcsine PSI & 31 & 37 & 15\\
Moderated paired $t$ on arcsine PSI & 20 & 31 & 12\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

For each null replicate, one sign is drawn per subject and reused across every event and cell-type contrast in that subject's fold. The variance prior is refit in each replicate. Null rejection for exact signed-rank testing is 0.0494 and 0.0516 at nominal 0.05, and 0.00107 and 0.00106 at nominal 0.001, in the two folds. The normal approximation rejects only 0.00042 and 0.00050 at nominal 0.001. Across 32 complete null families, exact testing makes at most two fold-0 and one fold-1 gene BH calls, but no replicated gene; none of the other seven statistics makes a replicated gene either. These synchronized diagnostics retain dependence across events and contrasts, although they still rely on sign symmetry and provide only 32 whole-family realizations. The discrepancy between good long-read directional enrichment and few split discoveries is partly a tail-calculation bug, but also reflects different endpoints, ranking selected events versus reproducible gene-level FDR discovery.

The corresponding primer-aware audit retains the original theta-allocation PSI, fold-specific primer offsets and dispersion, annotation cohort, and 2,191 matched gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses from 431 genes. The rebuilt effects and finite subject counts agree with the archived event tests. There are 4,267 and 4,270 matched event tests in the folds. Exact signed-rank tails give 13 and 16 fold-level gene BH calls and five replicated genes, versus six and six fold calls and no replicated gene under the normal approximation. Paired and moderated PSI-\(t\) tests each give four replicated genes, arcsine variants each give three, and logit variants each give two. Exact null rejection is 0.04867 and 0.04781 at nominal 0.05, and 0.000901 and 0.000907 at nominal 0.001. No statistic gives a replicated gene in any of the 32 synchronized subject-sign families. These results change the split comparison only; the full-data event and long-read rankings remain archived analyses.

For each covered gene--cell-type-pair context, let \(T_g\in\mathbb N\) be the number of EC-supported transcripts, indexed by \(t=1,\ldots,T_g\). The baseline \(\bar{\bm\theta}_g\in[0,1]^{T_g}\), with \(\bm 1^{\mathsf T}\bar{\bm\theta}_g=1\), is estimated by maximizing the primer-conditioned multinomial EC likelihood after pooling the counts over all covered pseudobulks from the two compared cell types. This fit ignores their cell-type labels and is repeated within each subject fold; context and fold indices are suppressed here. A SUPPA2 event \(e\) partitions these transcripts into included, excluded, and other-isoform sets \(G_{e,+}\), \(G_{e,-}\), and \(G_{e,0}\). An event is retained only when both tested sets are nonempty and every annotated included or excluded transcript is present in the EC-supported transcript set. For each nonempty class \(k\), define \(W_{e,tk}=\mathbf 1(t\in G_{e,k})\bar\theta_{gt}/\sum_{s\in G_{e,k}}\bar\theta_{gs}\). If a class has zero fitted abundance, its within-class weights are set uniformly to \(1/|G_{e,k}|\); event-fitting baseline probabilities are clipped away from zero. Thus \(W_e\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{T_g\times K_e}\), where \(K_e\in\{2,3\}\), with columns ordered \(+,-,0\). With the primer-specific compatibility matrix \(M_{gp}\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{E_{gp}\times T_g}\) for \(E_{gp}\) ECs, the collapsed map is \(M_{gep}=M_{gp}W_e\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{E_{gp}\times K_e}\). The observed EC counts remain uncollapsed; only the fixed transcript mixtures within event classes are combined.

For subject \(u\) and cell type \(c\in\{a,b\}\), define the conditional inclusion proportion \(\psi_{euc}\in(0,1)\) and the event's combined inclusion-plus-exclusion abundance \(m_{euc}\in(0,1)\). The corrected class-abundance vector is \(\bm a_{euc}(\psi,m)=(m_{euc}\psi_{euc},m_{euc}(1-\psi_{euc}),1-m_{euc})^{\mathsf T}\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{K_e}\), omitting its third coordinate and setting \(m_{euc}=1\) when the other-isoform set is empty. The transcript vector is \(\bm\theta_{euc}(\psi,m)=W_e\bm a_{euc}(\psi,m)\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{T_g}\). Within-class mixtures remain fixed, but event abundance is fitted separately in each subject and cell type as a nuisance parameter. For primer \(p\), the subject's pseudobulk counts are summed separately to give \(\bm y_{ucp}\in\mathbb N^{E_{gp}}\) and \(N_{ucp}=\bm 1^{\mathsf T}\bm y_{ucp}\). The EC mass is \(\bm r_{eucp}(\psi,m)=M_{gep}\bm a_{euc}(\psi,m)\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^{E_{gp}}\), and the log likelihood is \(\ell_{\rm EC}(\psi,m)=\sum_p\{\bm y_{ucp}^{\mathsf T}\log\bm r_{eucp}(\psi,m)-N_{ucp}\log[\bm 1^{\mathsf T}\bm r_{eucp}(\psi,m)]\}\), with elementwise logarithms. The estimate maximizes \(\ell_{\rm EC}(\psi,m)+\frac{\zeta}{2}[\log\psi+\log(1-\psi)]\), jointly over inclusion and event-mass logits. Here \(\zeta\) is the total smoothing pseudocount, split equally between the two tested classes; no additional event-mass prior is imposed. Profiling this nuisance abundance does not increase the degrees of freedom of the downstream inclusion contrast, which remains one.

The former fit held \(m_{euc}\) at its pooled value \(\bar m_e=\sum_{t\in G_{e,+}\cup G_{e,-}}\bar\theta_{gt}\). This can force changes in other-isoform abundance into the inclusion estimate when their ECs overlap an event class. A regression test uses a three-class EC map with columns \((1,0,0)^{\mathsf T}\), \((0,1,0)^{\mathsf T}\), and \((0,0.8,0.2)^{\mathsf T}\), and changes the true class abundance from \((0.48,0.32,0.20)^{\mathsf T}\) to \((0.16,0.04,0.80)^{\mathsf T}\). True inclusion rises from 0.6 to 0.8, but the fixed-mass fit estimates the opposite direction; profiling mass recovers the generating proportions. This is a structural misspecification, not an event-orientation convention error. Fixed within-class mixtures remain an approximation and can still distort estimates if isoforms within the same event class change their relative abundance.

The corrected event optimizer uses absolute inclusion logits for both two-class and three-class events. Bounds on a perturbation relative to a nearly zero pooled inclusion estimate can otherwise exclude supported posterior optima. A two-class regression test with pooled inclusion \(10^{-12}\), counts \((800,200)\), and total pseudocount 64 recovers inclusion \((800+32)/(1000+64)\). The pooled isoform baseline is fitted with analytic EC-likelihood gradients rather than finite differences. This retains its intended likelihood while reducing numerical-gradient sensitivity; the archived fixed-mass fit and an initial unchanged-model rerun differed numerically, so the diagnostic controls were rerun with the corrected baseline implementation. Conditional Fisher information is also evaluated using square-root EC-mass scaling to avoid overflow for tiny positive masses. These are numerical changes, separate from profiling nuisance abundance and separating testing from reporting.

The subject effect is \(d_{eu}=\operatorname{logit}\widehat\psi_{eu,b}-\operatorname{logit}\widehat\psi_{eu,a}\in\mathbb R\). The paired test uses \(d_{eu}/\sqrt{2}\), the included-minus-excluded ILR coordinate in cell type \(b\) minus that in cell type \(a\); each subject's sign agrees with an inclusion-PSI increase from \(a\) to \(b\). Conditional fitting covariances are computed but are not propagated into this hybrid's test. The split benchmark uses the ordinary paired \(t\) statistic with fixed \(\zeta=32\). The full-data fit uses fixed \(\zeta=64\) and additionally moderates scalar residual variances across tests with an empirical-Bayes variance prior. These concentrations are inherited from the Tealeaf analyses and are not re-selected by EB on the hybrid event family. Each test has 32 null realizations obtained by independently flipping its subject differences; the random seed depends on the event and contrast, so flips are not shared across different tests. The full-data variance prior is re-estimated in each null realization. Analytic tails are calibrated against pooled sign-flip tails in strata defined by paired-subject count, with each test's own null values excluded from its calibration pool and small strata pooled. Event p-values are combined by Simes within gene--cell-type pair, then across pairs within gene, before applying the two-fold conjunction and gene-level BH procedure for the split comparison. Full-data BH instead applies across converged event--contrast tests. Because flips are independent across tests, the null audit does not preserve the observed dependence among overlapping event hypotheses.

Reported inclusion effects are separate descriptive estimates. Let \(\widetilde\psi_{euc}\in(0,1)\) be an event-mass-profiled refit using total reporting pseudocount 1 and let \(\mathcal U_e\) be the subjects with valid refits in both cell types. We report \(\widehat{\Delta\psi}_e=|\mathcal U_e|^{-1}\sum_{u\in\mathcal U_e}(\widetilde\psi_{eu,b}-\widetilde\psi_{eu,a})\in[-1,1]\) for the short-read direction in the long-read audit. Test statistics, the variance prior, null calibration, and significance ranks still use the original testing fits. The fixed-mass reporting control raises top-200 directional agreement from 46.0\% to 51.0\%, without changing the BH discovery set or top-200 significance ranking. Analytic testing p-values agree within numerical tolerance; one calibrated tail differs by a single empirical-null count because of floating-point tie ordering, and a few near-zero effects show small changes in their unmoderated statistic ratios. Mean logit and mean PSI effects need not have the same sign across heterogeneous subjects; the reporting convention matches SUPPA2's mean paired PSI estimand more closely, but the source long-read direction remains a pooled PSI difference rather than a paired mean.

Across the two folds, 826 and 623 SUPPA2 event definitions were mappable to the EC-supported transcript models, yielding 4,651 and 3,257 converged event-by-cell-type-pair tests. The empirical null rejection rates were 0.050 at \(p<0.05\), 0.010 at \(p<0.01\), and 0.001 at \(p<0.001\) in each fold; none of the 32 null families produced a BH event discovery. On the 1,755 gene--cell-type-pair hypotheses shared with direct Tealeaf in both halves, the corrected hybrid produced 83 and 81 fold-level gene discoveries and 49 replicated genes, compared with 114 and 111 discoveries and 71 replicated genes for the selected concentration-32 direct Tealeaf procedure restricted to those same pairs. Held-half nominal replication and split log-p correlation were 0.884 and 0.646 for the hybrid, versus 0.876 and 0.792 for the matched current direct-path reference. The earlier direct-path values, 41/50 fold discoveries and 17 replicated genes, came from deprecated cross-fitted EB smoothing rather than the current selected estimator. The former fixed-mass hybrid fit gave 51 replicated genes, 0.887 held-half replication, and 0.665 correlation. Correcting nuisance abundance leaves the hybrid result similar, but correcting its reference removes the apparent replicated-discovery advantage over production Tealeaf. The mappable-event restriction is substantial. Empirical null rates are measured on the calibration pool, and independent test-specific flips do not reproduce dependence across events; these diagnostics therefore do not independently establish family-level FDR control.

The expanded full-data scan evaluates all seven SUPPA2 event categories against the gene-wide EC-supported transcript sets, subject to the EC-count and paired-subject coverage filters. The corrected fit produced 7,182 converged event--contrast tests for 1,114 mappable events in 6,695 covered gene--contrast contexts, with 1,247 test-level BH calls at \(\zeta=64\), compared with 1,417 for the former fixed-mass fit. Across the 32 independently generated sign-flip realizations, empirically calibrated rejection rates were 0.050, 0.010, and 0.001 at nominal 0.05, 0.01, and 0.001, with no BH discoveries. These rates are evaluated using the same pooled null distribution used for calibration, so agreement with nominal thresholds is not an independent calibration validation. For independent directional assessment, every converged hybrid test is mapped directly to the Tilgner transcript counts with no SUPPA2-ranked event restriction. Both inclusion and exclusion classes must have source transcript representation, both compared cell types must be present in each biological replicate, and the same source depth rules used for the other event methods are applied. The corrected hybrid's own top-200 pooled agreement is 104/200 (52.0\%). Its restricted event universe and fixed within-class mixtures remain limitations. A sensitivity replacing the ILR test with a paired PSI test on reporting fits, with the same variance moderation and sign-flip calibration, reduced top-200 agreement to 45.0\% for profiled mass and 44.5\% for fixed mass; it is not used for the reported hybrid comparison.

Reducing the corrected hybrid's testing concentration from 64 to 4, while keeping descriptive PSI refits at concentration 1, gives 105/200 (52.5\%) top-200 long-read agreement and 889 full-data BH calls, versus 104/200 (52.0\%) and 1,247 calls at concentration 64. This one-association difference in the external rank audit does not motivate changing the established testing concentration. The concentration-64 ILR test, concentration-1 reporting refit, and profiled event mass are used in the reported hybrid results; the weaker-testing and PSI-testing variants remain sensitivity analyses.

\end{document}
